## What #545 is, and what this repair is (plain engineering language)

#545 changed five register arms of the DC21285 "footbridge" device model.  Before it, a guest WRITE to
any of four read-only status registers (IRQ_STATUS +0x180, IRQ_RAW_STATUS +0x184, FIQ_STATUS +0x280,
FIQ_RAW_STATUS +0x284) or a guest READ of the write-only IRQEnableClear (+0x18c) ended the emulator process.
After it, each such access is DECLINED -- no register changes, a declined read returns 0 -- and ANNOUNCED
ONCE per site through a per-site warn-once latch (`warned_*`).  The detector is two instruments:
`regress/diff_footbridge.c`, an offline differential that `#include`s the real `dev_footbridge.c` and calls
its access function directly, and `regress/footbridge_sites_probe.py`, a 37-row guest probe.

The round has had pass 1, pass 2, an adjudication pass (§F2) and a regress/adjudication pass by the
flagship seat.  That last pass predicted, BY READING, that five one-condition edits to the DIRECTION test
of the edited arms would leave every row green:

    Q1  IRQ_STATUS        `if (writeflag == MEM_READ)`  ->  `if (writeflag == MEM_READ && d->irq_enable)`
    Q2  IRQ_RAW_STATUS    `if (writeflag == MEM_READ)`  ->  `if (writeflag == MEM_READ && d->irq_status)`
    Q3  FIQ_STATUS        `if (writeflag == MEM_READ)`  ->  `if (writeflag == MEM_READ && d->fiq_enable)`
    Q4  FIQ_RAW_STATUS    `if (writeflag == MEM_READ)`  ->  `if (writeflag == MEM_READ && d->fiq_status)`
    Q5  IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR  `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {`  ->  `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && d->irq_enable) {`

In the RESET state (every field 0, every latch clear) each sends a LEGAL access into the decline branch,
which announces a complaint that did not happen, consumes that site's latch (so the next real bad access
is silent) and -- for the reads -- returns 0, which is also the right value in that state.  The existing
rows could not see it: the latch-clear legal rows (X2, X5, X8) all run in a SEEDED state where the added
condition is true, and X7, which does run the all-zero state, latches every site before its legal access.

**MEASURED BEFORE THE REPAIR** (`regress/artifacts/fbd5/q2_20260923/Q2.log`, scratch trees, restored and
`cmp`-verified): FIX 333/333 offline + 37/37 guest; **Q1..Q5 each 333/333 and 37/37** -- all five confirmed.

**THE REPAIR (rows only; product code unchanged):**
* **X9**, 69 rows: every LEGAL access with the latches still CLEAR -- the five readable registers
  (IRQ_STATUS, IRQ_RAW_STATUS, FIQ_STATUS, FIQ_RAW_STATUS, and IRQEnable at +0x188) at widths 1, 2 and 4,
  plus the eight enable writes (Set/Clear x IRQ/FIQ x data 0 / 0x70) -- from three states: seeded
  (0x18/0x30/0x60/0xc0), all-zero, and wide (0x80010018/0x00010030/0x40020060/0x000200c0).  Each cell is ONE
  row on a fresh instance: returns 1, announces nothing, reads its value over the width (or writes the
  targeted enable as written, leaves the other three fields, and for IRQ Set/Clear drives the interrupt
  output exactly once by `irq_status & new_enable`, for FIQ not at all); then the five declines must
  announce EXACTLY 5, because no latch was consumed.
* **A WIDE third state in X6 and X7** (+54, +35 rows): every earlier seed fit in one byte, so an edit
  keyed on bits 8-31 of a field passed every row (the flagship seat's "(e) cell").
* IDENTITY 333 -> 491; `gate_offline.sh` pins moved to 491 / 311 / 162 / 105 / 6 / 69 (distinct green names).

**PRETABLE_Q2.out** was written at 14:19:25 and never amended; the repair table started 14:22:42.

**MEASURED AFTER THE REPAIR** (`q2_20260923/repair/Q2R.log`, one frozen instrument, md5 ff8f87b6):

    FIX  491 rows, 0 failures
    Q1   3 red: X9 legal len 1/2/4 read of IRQ_STATUS (all-zero, latches clear)
    Q2   3 red: X9 ... IRQ_RAW_STATUS (all-zero)      Q3  3 red: X9 ... FIQ_STATUS (all-zero)
    Q4   3 red: X9 ... FIQ_RAW_STATUS (all-zero)      Q5  2 red: X9 IRQ Clear write 0 / 0x70 (all-zero)
    W1   12 red, all "X6 write IRQ_STATUS ... wide first/repeat"   (control: decline body also does
         `d->irq_status &= 0xffff;` -- invisible at byte seeds)
    W2   3 red, all "X7 legal len 1/2/4 read of IRQ_STATUS (wide) re-arms NO latch"   (control: the
         IRQ_STATUS read branch also clears its latch when `d->irq_status >> 16`)
    T1, T2 (the earlier width controls)  2 red each
    59 variants compared row-by-row against the 327-row TABLE4 (`q2_20260923/repair/CARRIED_KILLS.txt`):
    0 lost; one historic ROW RENAME is mapped, not counted (the 333-row instrument renamed an X3 row).
    Every prediction in PRETABLE_Q2.out matched exactly.  Guest-only variants (M9, Z9 announce TEXT; F1
    `&& !timer_control[0]`) are 0 offline before and after, by design.

## §C — SETTLED PREMISES (recorded resolutions; do not re-derive, but say if the diff contradicts one)

1. **The product change is settled** -- decline + once-latched announce at the five sites, the value 0 for
   the declined read; pass 1, pass 2, §F2 and the adjudication all ruled on it; this repair does not touch it.
2. **The DEPENDS-ON bound these rows answer to** (adjudication pass, Q1/D1): the predicates DOMINATING each
   edited decline, walked to the function boundary -- the case label, the direction test, the latch -- and
   the lvalues read by BOTH branches under the direction test: `irq_status`, `irq_enable`, `fiq_status`,
   `fiq_enable` and the five latches.
3. **Insertion-class edits in OTHER cases** (a writer added at +0x188, the FIQ enable pair, TIMER_1_VALUE)
   are outside that closure (adjudication Q1 narrowed them); their existing rows stay, ungraded as floor.
4. **The guest reset-state reads the flagship seat RECOMMENDED** (+0x180/+0x184/+0x280/+0x284 at the head of
   the probe's arm S, scored on a buffer mark) are NOT added -- the floor is carried offline by X9, and arm
   S keeps its disclosed "order, not emitter" limit.  Q5 below asks whether that is right.

## THE HANDLER ARMS (dev_footbridge.c, unchanged by this repair; lines 566-660 of the frozen copy)

```c
	case UART_FLAGS:
		odata = UART_TX_EMPTY;
		break;

	case IRQ_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->irq_status & d->irq_enable;
		else if (!d->warned_irq_status_w) {
			/*  #545: read-only register (7.3.29); the write is
			    declined -- no register changes, only this site's
			    warn-once latch is set -- and announced once.  */
			d->warned_irq_status_w = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to irq status? "
			    "(once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_RAW_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->irq_status;
		else if (!d->warned_irq_raw_w) {
			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.30); declined, announced once.  */
			d->warned_irq_raw_w = 1;
			fatal("[ footbridge write to irq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_ENABLE_SET:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->irq_enable |= idata;
			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
			else
				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
		} else {
			/*  #539: a read of 188h is documented -- IRQEnable is
			    "read-only ... can only be changed by writing to
			    the EnableSet and EnableClear registers", reset 0
			    (DC21285 Table 7-10, 7.3.31).  The value below was
			    always right; the exit(1) that followed it is gone.  */
			odata = d->irq_enable;
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->irq_enable &= ~idata;
			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
			else
				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
		} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {
			/*  #545: IRQEnableClear is write-only (7.3.33) and Table
			    7-10 names no register at 18Ch for reads, so the read
			    is declined with 0 -- the IRQEnable value that used to
			    be computed here is 188h's answer, not 18Ch's.  */
			d->warned_irq_clear_r = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge read from "
			    "ENABLE CLEAR? (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->fiq_status & d->fiq_enable;
		else if (!d->warned_fiq_status_w) {
			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.29); declined, announced once.  */
			d->warned_fiq_status_w = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to fiq status? "
			    "(once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_RAW_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->fiq_status;
		else if (!d->warned_fiq_raw_w) {
			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.30); declined, announced once.  */
			d->warned_fiq_raw_w = 1;
			fatal("[ footbridge write to fiq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_ENABLE_SET:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->fiq_enable |= idata;
		break;

	case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->fiq_enable &= ~idata;
		break;

	case TIMER_1_LOAD:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
```

## §E -- QUESTIONS (answer each, numbered)

**Q1 -- premise audit.**  I state these as facts; check each rather than agreeing:
 (a) ACCESS {declined, legal} x STATE {seeded, all-zero, wide} x LATCH {clear, set} at the five sites is
     the whole class under the §C.2 bound, and X6 (declined x both latches), X7 (legal x set) and X9
     (legal x clear) cover it -- walk the predicates that dominate each edited decline in the inlined
     handler and say whether any is missing;
 (b) X9's expectations match the handler: for each of the eight enable writes, work out the resulting
     enable field and whether the handler calls ASSERT or DEASSERT (or neither), from the inlined code, in
     all three states, and compare with the rule the X9 row asserts;
 (c) the wide seeds exercise bits above byte 0 in every one of the four fields, and the masked reads
     (IRQ_STATUS = irq_status & irq_enable; FIQ_STATUS = fiq_status & fiq_enable) are non-zero above byte
     0 in the wide state -- ENUMERATE the four products;
 (d) no pre-repair row changed its meaning (row names are unchanged; X6/X7 only gain cells).
**Q2 -- the smallest change that the 491 rows cannot see.**  Name one change to the five handler arms (or to
the detector) that re-opens what the declines protect -- a legal access treated as a bad one, a bad one
not announced, a field changed, a latch consumed or re-armed -- while every one of the 491 rows stays green,
and name the row you expect to stay green.  (Examples of the SHAPE, not claims: a condition keyed on one
exact field value, on a field bit no state sets, or on a latch belonging to a different site.)
**Q3 -- class or instance.**  Two consecutive passes (§F2, then the adjudication) found cells of one space.
The carrier's rule then owes the CLASS in one diff, with the contract naming what is covered and what is
not.  Does this repair close the class or only the five instances?  Name any cell of site x direction x
width x data x state x latch that is NOT rowed, and say whether it is inside the §C.2 bound.
**Q4 -- records.**  Is any sentence in the added comments (the X9 header, the rewritten X6/X7 CONTRACT, the
gate_offline.sh comment) false as of today, or stale the moment it lands?
**Q5 -- the guest row.**  Is leaving the guest reset-state reads out (§C.4) right, given the offline rows
carry the floor -- or does the guest instrument owe them for a reason the offline rows cannot cover?

If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE -- say so.

End with a seat record: model, what you read, what you ran.


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

    5:  *  a guest-writable zero load is an integer division by zero, reproduced independently by two
    6:  *  seats as SIGFPE (exit 136).  A crash fix with no detector is this project's fifth vacuity
    7:  *  class, and here the detector had to be offline for a reason stronger than convenience:
    8:  *  THERE IS NO NETWINDER OR CATS RIG ANYWHERE IN regress/ -- nothing in this tree boots a
    9:  *  machine that has a footbridge.  So this is not the cheapest detector, it is the ONLY
   10:  *  possible one.

   14:  *  the feasibility question answerable at all.  Nothing is transcribed.

   18:  *  machine_add_tickfunction, memory_device_register).  The trade-off, stated so it is not

  140: 
  141: 	/*  LINE-BUFFERED ON PURPOSE.  gate_offline.sh redirects this binary's stdout to a file,
  142: 	    which makes it FULLY buffered -- so when a mutant re-introduces the crash, the log is
  143: 	    0 BYTES and the operator gets a red row with nothing in it.  Measured: line-buffered,
  144: 	    the same run leaves 943 bytes naming A1/B2/D1 as FAIL before it dies.  The gate's
  145: 	    greps fail either way, so this is legibility rather than detection -- but it is this
  146: 	    project's own recorded stdout-buffering trap, in a new place.  */
  147: 	setvbuf(stdout, NULL, _IOLBF, 0);

  154: 
  155: 	{
  156: 		/*
  157: 		 *  SELFCHECK -- CAN THIS FILE'S COMPARATOR STILL FAIL?
  158: 		 *
  159: 		 *  Feed check() deliberate mismatches and require the failure counter to move,
  160: 		 *  then un-record them so the rows are free.  If check() has been stubbed,
  161: 		 *  neutered, or had its comparison edited away, EVERY other row in this file is
  162: 		 *  silently inert and the gate cannot tell -- measured: 118 rows across five
  163: 		 *  detectors stayed green under exactly that stub, row counts, identity rows and
  164: 		 *  verdict tokens all intact.
  165: 		 *
  166: 		 *  TWO MISMATCHES, ONE ALPHABETIC AND ONE DIGIT-LEADING, and the second is not
  167: 		 *  padding.  The first version fed only "a" vs "b", and a pass-2 seat defeated it
  168: 		 *  with one line -- `isdigit(got[0]) || strcmp(...) == 0` -- because EVERY REAL ROW
  169: 		 *  IN THIS FILE COMPARES NUMBERS.  118 rows went inert, this sentinel stayed blind,
  170: 		 *  and four of the five gate-2 self-mutants stayed green too.  A sentinel whose
  171: 		 *  input does not resemble the rows it vouches for is a sentinel with a whitelist.
  172: 		 *
  173: 		 *  THE HEALTHY PATH PRINTS AN ok-SHAPED LINE, DELIBERATELY.  The first version
  174: 		 *  printed nothing on success and a FAIL line only on failure, which broke two
  175: 		 *  things at once: there was no stable string for gate 2 to grep, so DELETING THIS
  176: 		 *  WHOLE BLOCK FROM ALL FIVE FILES left the gate at PASS with the same 220 checks
  177: 		 *  and zero red rows -- and the FAIL text it printed on the HEALTHY path polluted
  178: 		 *  the gate's kill attribution, so a self-mutant pinned to a row id like "fail"
  179: 		 *  matched this line and reported OK for a mutant nothing had caught.
  180: 		 *
  181: 		 *  `>` not `== +1`: a comparator that double-counts compares perfectly well, and
  182: 		 *  the exact form printed "no longer compares" at it.  Direction was safe, the
  183: 		 *  diagnosis was wrong.
  184: 		 *
  185: 		 *  This does NOT prove any row is correct.  It proves the comparator still fails on
  186: 		 *  inputs shaped like the ones the rows use.  A comparator that compares
  187: 		 *  SELECTIVELY -- or that special-cases this row by name -- is still invisible to
  188: 		 *  it, and only a census would see that.  Liveness sentinel, nothing more.
  189: 		 *
  190: 		 *  THE PROBE ROWS ARE NAMED @@SELFCHECK@@ SO THEY CANNOT BE MISTAKEN FOR A ROW.
  191: 		 *  They are deliberate mismatches, so check() prints FAIL for them on the HEALTHY
  192: 		 *  path -- and a pass-2 seat measured that a self-mutant pinned to a row id like
  193: 		 *  "fail" matched one of those lines and reported OK for a mutant nothing caught.
  194: 		 *  The obvious cure is worse than the disease: suppressing the print INSIDE check()
  195: 		 *  would need a name test in the comparator, which is precisely the whitelist the
  196: 		 *  same seat showed defeats this sentinel (`strncmp(name,"SELFCHECK",9)`).  So the
  197: 		 *  comparator is left alone and the probe rows carry a token no real row would use;
  198: 		 *  selfmutant.py filters it, and gate 2 greps the SUMMARY line below for presence.
  199: 		 */
  200: 		int selfcheck_f = fails, selfcheck_r = rows, selfcheck_bad = 0;
  201: 
  202: 		check("@@SELFCHECK@@/str", "a", "b");
  203: 		if (fails <= selfcheck_f)
  204: 			selfcheck_bad++;
  205: 		fails = selfcheck_f; rows = selfcheck_r;
  206: 
  207: 		check("@@SELFCHECK@@/num", "1", "2");
  208: 		if (fails <= selfcheck_f)
  209: 			selfcheck_bad++;
  210: 		fails = selfcheck_f; rows = selfcheck_r;
  211: 
  212: 		if (selfcheck_bad) {
  213: 			printf("  FAIL  SELFCHECK the comparator no longer compares (%d of 2 "
  214: 			    "mismatches went unnoticed) -- every row in this file is inert\n",
  215: 			    selfcheck_bad);
  216: 			fails = selfcheck_f + 1;
  217: 			rows = selfcheck_r;
  218: 		} else {
  219: 			printf("  ok    SELFCHECK the comparator can still fail       "
  220: 			    "str+num\n");
  221: 		}
  222: 	}
  223: 
  224: 	printf("--- A. the load interpretation: zero is a FULL PERIOD, never a divisor of 0 ---\n");
  225: 	/*  16777216 == 2^24, written as an absolute rather than read from TIMER_MAX_VAL: a row
  226: 	    that takes its expected value from the code under test cannot notice the code
  227: 	    changing (the `constblind` finding).  */
  228: 	check_u("A1 load 0 becomes the full 24-bit span",
  229: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0, 0, 0), 16777216);
  230: 	check_u("A2 an ordinary load is untouched",
  231: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(1000, 0, 0), 1000);
  232: 	check_u("A3 the maximum load is untouched",
  233: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0xffffff, 0, 0), 16777215);
  234: 
  235: 	printf("--- B. the prescaler is 64-bit MULTIPLICATION, not a signed shift ---\n");
  236: 	/*  THE ROW THAT CATCHES THE DEFECT THE OBVIOUS FIX RE-CREATES.  The old code used
  237: 	    `int cycles` with `cycles <<= 8`; three seats found independently that this
  238: 	    overflows for exactly half the legal range, and that mapping zero to 2^24 while
  239: 	    leaving `int` gives 2^24 << 8 == 2^32 == 0 -> +INF again.  B1 and B2 are the two
  240: 	    halves of that: the reset load prescaled, and the zero load prescaled.  */
  241: 	check_u("B1 reset load x256 does not overflow",
  242: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0xffffff, TIMER_FCLK_256, 1),
  243: 	    (uint64_t) 16777215 * 256);
  244: 	check_u("B2 zero load x256 does not wrap to zero",
  245: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0, TIMER_FCLK_256, 1),
  246: 	    (uint64_t) 16777216 * 256);
  247: 	check_u("B3 the x16 prescaler", footbridge_effective_cycles(1000, TIMER_FCLK_16, 1),
  248: 	    16000);
  249: 
  250: 	printf("--- C. TIMER_EXTERNAL is a LEGAL encoding and must not kill the host ---\n");
  251: 	/*  Both prescaler bits set is TIMER_EXTERNAL (0x0C).  The order of the tests matters:
  252: 	    with a bare `else if` chain, 0x0C reads as FCLK_16 by accident.  */
  253: 	check_u("C1 TIMER_EXTERNAL is not silently read as x16",
  254: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(1000, TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256, 1), 1000);
  255: 
  256: 	printf("--- D. THE TWO CONSUMERS LIVE IN DIFFERENT DOMAINS ---\n");
  257: 	/*  THE ROW A TENTH READING FORCED.  timer_value is a 24-bit GUEST-READABLE register.
  258: 	    Feeding it the PRESCALED span would store values far above 24 bits -- the measuring
  259: 	    seat's own first proposal did exactly that and put 19833 of 20000 ticks out of
  260: 	    range.  The prescaler slows the RATE; it does not widen the COUNTER.  */
  261: 	check_u("D1 the counter domain ignores the prescaler",
  262: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0, TIMER_FCLK_256, 0), 16777216);
  263: 	{
  264: 		int i, over = 0;
  265: 		arm_irqs(d);
  266: 		d->timer_load[0] = 0;
  267: 		d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256;
  268: 		d->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 1;
  269: 		for (i = 0; i < 20000; i++) {
  270: 			dev_footbridge_tick(cpu, d);
  271: 			if (d->timer_value[0] > 0xffffff)
  272: 				over++;
  273: 		}
  274: 		check_u("D2 20000 ticks leave the 24-bit counter in range", over, 0);
  275: 	}
  276: 	/*  D3 EXISTS BECAUSE D2 WAS NOT ENOUGH, and the review measured why: every tick-path
  277: 	    row above uses load == 0, and for load == 0 the constant 16777216 is exactly the
  278: 	    right answer -- so replacing the whole helper call with that literal passed the
  279: 	    entire file.  The counter must TRACK THE GUEST'S LOAD, not merely stay in range.  */
  280: 	{
  281: 		int i, over = 0;
  282: 		struct footbridge_data *e = calloc(1, sizeof(*e));
  283: 		arm_irqs(e);
  284: 		e->timer_load[0] = 1000;
  285: 		e->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
  286: 		e->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 1;
  287: 		for (i = 0; i < 20000; i++) {
  288: 			dev_footbridge_tick(cpu, e);
  289: 			if (e->timer_value[0] >= 1000)
  290: 				over++;
  291: 		}
  292: 		check_u("D3 the counter stays below the guest's OWN load", over, 0);
  293: 		free(e);
  294: 	}
  295: 
  296: 	printf("--- E. the rate the device asks the timer core for ---\n");
  297: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(*d));
  298: 	d->timer_load[0] = 1000;
  299: 	d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
  300: 	last_freq = -1.0;
  301: 	reload_timer_value(cpu, d, 0);
  302: 	check_u("E1 a 1000-cycle load at 100 MHz asks for 100 kHz",
  303: 	    (uint64_t) (last_freq + 0.5), 100000);
  304: 	/*  E2 is the route that needs NO load write at all: the reset load is TIMER_MAX_VAL,
  305: 	    so one write of ENABLE|FCLK_256 reaches the old overflow.  Before #432 this asked
  306: 	    for a NEGATIVE rate, which the core's floor clamp turned into one tick per ~3.2
  307: 	    years, silently.  */
  308: 	d->timer_load[0] = 0xffffff;
  309: 	d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256;
  310: 	last_freq = -1.0;
  311: 	reload_timer_value(cpu, d, 0);
  312: 	check(  "E2 the reset load x256 asks for a POSITIVE rate",
  313: 	    last_freq > 0.0 ? "positive" : "NEGATIVE OR ZERO", "positive");
  314: 	/*  E3: a zero load must ask for a slow, SERVICEABLE rate -- not the domain ceiling.
  315: 	    100e6 / 2^24 is about 5.96 Hz.  */
  316: 	d->timer_load[0] = 0;
  317: 	d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
  318: 	last_freq = -1.0;
  319: 	reload_timer_value(cpu, d, 0);
  320: 	check(  "E3 a zero load asks for a few Hz, not the ceiling",
  321: 	    (last_freq > 1.0 && last_freq < 100.0) ? "serviceable" : "OUT OF RANGE",
  322: 	    "serviceable");
  323: 	/*  E4 EXISTS BECAUSE E2 WAS A SIGN-ONLY ORACLE, and the review measured the cost of
  324: 	    that: changing the `1` in reload_timer_value()'s helper call to a `0` -- ONE
  325: 	    CHARACTER -- silently deletes the prescaler from the RATE domain for every guest,
  326: 	    and all fifteen rows stayed green.  Sections B and C prove the HELPER prescales;
  327: 	    D1/D2 prove the COUNTER consumer does not; nothing pinned the RATE consumer to
  328: 	    prescaling, which is the round's headline design decision.  100 MHz / (1000 * 256)
  329: 	    is 390.625 Hz; the mutant asks for 100000, a rate 256x too fast.  */
  330: 	d->timer_load[0] = 1000;
  331: 	d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256;
  332: 	last_freq = -1.0;
  333: 	reload_timer_value(cpu, d, 0);
  334: 	check_u("E4 the RATE consumer really applies the x256 prescaler",
  335: 	    (uint64_t) (last_freq * 1000.0 + 0.5), 390625);
  336: 
  337: 	printf("--- F. END TO END: the shipped tick survives route 2, in a forked child ---\n");
  338: 	/*  ROUTE 2, the one that PREDATES #427/#428 and is the reason this round does not get
  339: 	    to blame its own hardening: TIMER_x_LOAD does not clear pending_timer_interrupts
  340: 	    (TIMER_x_CONTROL does), so an ordinary legal timer accrues a backlog and a later
  341: 	    write of zero divides.  Forked, because a mutant that re-crashes would otherwise
  342: 	    take the whole gate binary down and score as a census FAULT rather than a named-row
  343: 	    KILL.  */
  344: 	{
  345: 		pid_t pid = fork();
  346: 		if (pid == 0) {
  347: 			struct footbridge_data *c = calloc(1, sizeof(*c));
  348: 			int i;
  349: 			arm_irqs(c);
  350: 			c->timer_load[0] = 50000;	/*  an ordinary legal timer  */
  351: 			c->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
  352: 			for (i = 0; i < 400; i++)
  353: 				c->pending_timer_interrupts[0]++;
  354: 			c->timer_load[0] = 0;		/*  the guest writes zero  */
  355: 			dev_footbridge_tick(cpu, c);
  356: 			_exit(0);
  357: 		} else {
  358: 			int st = 0;
  359: 			waitpid(pid, &st, 0);
  360: 			check("F1 a zero load after a backlog does not kill the host",
  361: 			    WIFEXITED(st) && WEXITSTATUS(st) == 0 ? "survived"
  362: 			    : "KILLED BY SIGNAL", "survived");
  363: 		}
  364: 	}
  365: 
  366: 	printf("--- G. the CONTROL write path: a legal encoding must not exit() ---\n");
  367: 	/*  THE ROW THAT DEFENDS THE FOLD.  Section C proves the helper INTERPRETS 0x0C; this
  368: 	    proves the guest can actually WRITE it.  Before #432 this exact write reached
  369: 	    fatal()+exit(1) in the CONTROL arm -- a guest selecting the external clock source
  370: 	    killed the host, and no amount of helper correctness would have saved it.  Forked,
  371: 	    because a mutant that restores the exit(1) would otherwise take the gate binary
  372: 	    down and score as a census FAULT instead of a named-row KILL.  */
  373: 	{
  374: 		pid_t pid = fork();
  375: 		if (pid == 0) {
  376: 			struct footbridge_data *c = calloc(1, sizeof(*c));
  377: 			unsigned char buf[4];
  378: 			arm_irqs(c);
  379: 			c->timer_load[0] = 1000;
  380: 			memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  381: 			buf[0] = TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256;
  382: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CONTROL, buf,
  383: 			    4, MEM_WRITE, c);
  384: 			_exit(0);
  385: 		} else {
  386: 			int st = 0;
  387: 			waitpid(pid, &st, 0);
  388: 			check("G1 a guest write of ENABLE|EXTERNAL does not exit()",
  389: 			    WIFEXITED(st) && WEXITSTATUS(st) == 0 ? "survived"
  390: 			    : "HOST KILLED", "survived");
  391: 		}
  392: 	}
  393: 
  394: 	printf("--- H. the LOAD register reads back EXACTLY what the guest wrote ---\n");
  395: 	/*  THE FIFTH-VACUITY-CLASS ROW, and the adjudicating seat found it by attacking its own
  396: 	    design: "timer_load[] is never rewritten -- the guest reads back exactly what it
  397: 	    wrote" is a shipped, load-bearing claim, and it had NO DETECTOR.  Reinstating
  398: 	    normalize-at-write -- `timer_load = idata ? idata : 1`, precisely the alternative
  399: 	    this round REJECTED -- passed all fifteen rows, because nothing in this file ever
  400: 	    drove TIMER_1_LOAD through the access handler; every other row pokes struct fields
  401: 	    directly.  These two rows go through dev_footbridge_access in both directions.  */
  402: 	{
  403: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(*h));
  404: 		unsigned char buf[4];
  405: 		uint64_t back;
  406: 		arm_irqs(h);
  407: 
  408: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  409: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
  410: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  411: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  412: 		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
  413: 		check_u("H1 a guest write of 0 reads back as 0, not normalised", back, 0);
  414: 
  415: 		/*  H2 pins the 24-bit MASK as well: 0x1000000 is "one full wrap", and the mask
  416: 		    aliases it to zero -- which is the arithmetic that makes the full-period
  417: 		    mapping the continuous choice in the first place.  */
  418: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  419: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x1000000);
  420: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
  421: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  422: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  423: 		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
  424: 		check_u("H2 0x1000000 is masked to 0, and stays 0 on read-back", back, 0);
  425: 		free(h);
  426: 	}
  427: 
  428: 	/*  fbclearread: `case TIMER_1_CLEAR:` carries NO writeflag test, while the case
  429: 	    immediately above it (TIMER_1_CONTROL) does.  So a guest READ runs the whole
  430: 	    body: it reloads the counter when periodic, decrements the pending count, and
  431: 	    DEASSERTS the interrupt.  A guest that merely reads the register silently
  432: 	    acknowledges a pending timer interrupt -- the likely guest-visible consequence
  433: 	    is a LOST interrupt, not a corrupted value.
  434: 
  435: 	    FOUR addresses, not one: the timer fold normalises 0x30C/0x32C/0x34C/0x36C onto
  436: 	    this single case.
  437: 
  438: 	    These rows drive the ACCESS HANDLER rather than poking struct fields, for the
  439: 	    reason this file already records above: a struct-poking row cannot see a
  440: 	    handler-level defect at all.  */
  441: 	{
  442: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(*h));
  443: 		unsigned char buf[4];
  444: 		uint64_t back;
  445: 
  446: 		arm_irqs(h);
  447: 
  448: 		/*  CONTROL first: a WRITE must decrement.  Without it a green defect row
  449: 		    could equally mean the handler never ran.  */
  450: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  451: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  452: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
  453: 		check_u("[LIVE] a WRITE of TIMER_1_CLEAR decrements pending",
  454: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 1);
  455: 
  456: 		/*  THE DEFECT: a READ must leave the pending count alone.  */
  457: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  458: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  459: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  460: 		/*  THE GUARD WRAPS THREE EFFECTS AND THE ROWS ABOVE OBSERVE TWO.
  461: 		    reload_timer_value and the pending decrement are both pinned;
  462: 		    INTERRUPT_DEASSERT was not.  A panel seat named the mutant that
  463: 		    exploits the gap -- hoist the deassert OUT of the guard so a READ
  464: 		    still deasserts:
  465: 
  466: 		        if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) { reload; pending--; }
  467: 		        INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
  468: 
  469: 		    Every other row passes -- the deassert touches neither the pending
  470: 		    count nor timer_value -- while the guest READ once again
  471: 		    acknowledges a pending interrupt.  **That is #474's headline
  472: 		    symptom, reintroduced**: the round's own record calls the
  473: 		    consequence a LOST interrupt, and a lost interrupt IS a spurious
  474: 		    deassert.
  475: 
  476: 		    The counter was already armed by noop_deassert and NEVER READ.
  477: 		    A counter nothing asserts on is a row that does not exist.  */
  478: 		/*  THE ACCEPT COMPANION, and the row below is HALF A ROW without it.
  479: 		    A row that wants ZERO can only fail UPWARD: delete
  480: 		    INTERRUPT_DEASSERT from the guarded body outright and the READ
  481: 		    row stays green, because zero deasserts is exactly what it
  482: 		    asserts.  Measured by a panel seat against the version that
  483: 		    shipped this row without its pair.
  484: 
  485: 		    This is the de-escalation clause's accept side in miniature:
  486: 		    the fix made a channel CONDITIONAL, so the detector owes a row
  487: 		    proving the channel still fires on the legal path.  */
  488: 		irq_deasserts = 0;
  489: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  490: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  491: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
  492: 		check_u("[LIVE] a WRITE DOES deassert the irq", irq_deasserts, 1);
  493: 
  494: 		irq_deasserts = 0;
  495: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  496: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  497: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  498: 		check_u("a READ must NOT deassert the irq", irq_deasserts, 0);
  499: 
  500: 		check_u("a READ of TIMER_1_CLEAR must NOT clear pending",
  501: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
  502: 
  503: 		/*  ENABLE.  A measuring seat named this the worst of five one-token
  504: 		    escapes: widen the guard with `|| (timer_control & TIMER_ENABLE)`
  505: 		    and it sweeps the whole table, because every other row here uses
  506: 		    control = 0 or PERIODIC and never TIMER_ENABLE.
  507: 
  508: 		    It is the worst because of WHICH state it re-opens: a DISABLED
  509: 		    timer can never hold a pending interrupt, so the mutant re-opens
  510: 		    the defect on precisely the state this round exists for.  */
  511: 		h->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
  512: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  513: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  514: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  515: 		check_u("an ENABLED timer READ does not clear",
  516: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
  517: 		h->timer_control[0] = 0;
  518: 
  519: 		/*  WIDTH.  `&& len == 4` sweeps every row above -- they all use a
  520: 		    four-byte access.  The same shape as the wdc detector's own width
  521: 		    hole, found the same day in a different file.  */
  522: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  523: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  524: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 2, MEM_READ, h);
  525: 		check_u("a 2-byte READ does not clear either",
  526: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
  527: 
  528: 		/*  *** THE ANCHOR ROWS, and the premise they correct is mine. ***
  529: 		    This round's record said 'every row exercises timer 1'.  It does
  530: 		    not: TIMER_1_CLEAR is 0x30C and (0x30C >> 5) & 3 == 0, so every
  531: 		    row above is an INDEX-0 row.  That is exactly why `timer_nr = 0`
  532: 		    and a widened fold mask each score a clean sweep -- the file
  533: 		    claims FOUR addresses and carried no row distinguishing any of
  534: 		    them.
  535: 
  536: 		    0x32C selects index 1 and 0x36C index 3.  Asserting the NEIGHBOUR
  537: 		    is untouched is what makes these ANCHORED rather than merely
  538: 		    distinct: a re-map that collides on one probed cell still reds.
  539: 		    */
  540: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 5;
  541: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[1] = 2;
  542: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  543: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_2_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
  544: 		check_u("0x32C decrements timer index 1",
  545: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[1], 1);
  546: 		check_u("...and leaves index 0 untouched",
  547: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 5);
  548: 
  549: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[3] = 2;
  550: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  551: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_4_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
  552: 		check_u("0x36C decrements timer index 3",
  553: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[3], 1);
  554: 		check_u("...and index 0 is still untouched",
  555: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 5);
  556: 
  557: 		/*  A READ AT A NON-ZERO INDEX, and it is the row the anchor pair
  558: 		    above could not be: those drive WRITES, so a guard widened by
  559: 		    `|| timer_nr != 0` passes them for the ordinary reason and
  560: 		    nothing reds.  MEASURED: that mutant escaped the 32-row table
  561: 		    until this row existed.
  562: 
  563: 		    The general shape is worth naming: an ANCHOR row proves WHICH
  564: 		    cell an access lands on, and a GUARD row proves the access is
  565: 		    refused.  A mutant that widens the guard per-index needs both
  566: 		    properties in ONE row, and neither existing row had them.  */
  567: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[1] = 2;
  568: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  569: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_2_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  570: 		check_u("a READ at index 1 does not clear",
  571: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[1], 2);
  572: 
  573: 		/*  THE COMMON TAIL, and it is the row the fix's own comment needed.
  574: 		    That comment says the `break` is deliberate because an early
  575: 		    `return` would skip
  576: 		        if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  577: 		                memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
  578: 		    and hand a guest an untouched buffer.  Nothing tested that: buf was
  579: 		    memset to 0xff and never read, so `break` -> `return 1` passed both
  580: 		    rows.  A stated rationale with no row behind it is a claim, not a
  581: 		    property.  Same access as the row above -- the question is what that
  582: 		    call LEFT IN THE BUFFER, not what it did to the device.
  583: 
  584: 		    Zero is the tail writing an untouched odata, which is also what the
  585: 		    pre-fix code returned; the datasheet specifies no read value for a
  586: 		    write-only register, so this pins DETERMINISM, not architecture.  */
  587: 		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
  588: 		check_u("a READ still runs the common tail", back, 0);
  589: 
  590: 		/*  PERIODIC READ.  The rows above use control = 0, so the periodic arm
  591: 		    of the guarded body never runs and a guard widened to admit a
  592: 		    periodic read still passes them.  reload_timer_value() assigns
  593: 		    timer_value = timer_load, so a value deliberately UNEQUAL to load
  594: 		    makes the reload observable with no clock and no tick.
  595: 
  596: 		    This is the clause-(d) row for this fix: it keeps the guard and
  597: 		    nulls its repair on the one path the other rows cannot reach.  */
  598: 		h->timer_control[0] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC;
  599: 		h->timer_load[0]    = 0x1234;
  600: 		h->timer_value[0]   = 0x9999;	/*  deliberately != load  */
  601: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  602: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  603: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  604: 		check_u("a PERIODIC read must not reload the counter",
  605: 		    h->timer_value[0], 0x9999);
  606: 		check_u("a PERIODIC read must not clear pending either",
  607: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
  608: 
  609: 		free(h);
  610: 	}
  611: 
  612: 	/*
  613: 	 *  #545 P-ROWS -- the five undefined-direction accesses that used to end the process are
  614: 	 *  DECLINED with a latched announce.  These rows pin what no stepped guest can (the guest
  615: 	 *  rows live in footbridge_sites_probe.py, arm S): PRESERVATION under a NONZERO status, the
  616: 	 *  read EXPRESSIONS, the widths, and the latch -- fatal() is a counter here, so the announce
  617: 	 *  is counted, never read (the misname check is the guest rows' job).
  618: 	 *
  619: 	 *  SEEDED BY DIRECT FIELD WRITE, NEVER THROUGH +0x188/+0x18c.  A bare calloc'd instance has
  620: 	 *  NULL d->irq callbacks and the enable arms call INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq), so this block
  621: 	 *  installs counting no-ops FIRST; the seeds are still written directly, so that no enable-
  622: 	 *  arm assert is mixed into the interrupt-output counts the rows below compare.
  623: 	 *  Seeds (enumerated, pass 2): 0x18/0x30/0x60/0xc0 -- every field-for-field swap in either
  624: 	 *  status expression yields a DIFFERENT value (0x50 collided: 0x50 & 0x30 == 0x18 & 0x30).
  625: 	 *  fiq_status has no writer in the product; seeding it pins the +0x280/+0x284 expressions
  626: 	 *  against a value the product never produces -- a NARROWING of that gap, not its closure.
  627: 	 */
  628: 	{
  629: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
  630: 		unsigned char buf[8];
  631: 		char nm[96], nm2[128];
  632: 		int f0, i;
  633: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; int len; const char *what; } dec[] = {
  634: 			{ IRQ_STATUS,       MEM_WRITE, 4, "P1 write IRQ_STATUS"       },
  635: 			{ IRQ_RAW_STATUS,   MEM_WRITE, 4, "P2 write IRQ_RAW_STATUS"   },
  636: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ,  4, "P3 read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR"  },
  637: 			{ FIQ_STATUS,       MEM_WRITE, 4, "P4 write FIQ_STATUS"       },
  638: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS,   MEM_WRITE, 4, "P5 write FIQ_RAW_STATUS"   },
  639: 		};
  640: 		arm_irqs(h);
  641: 		h->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
  642: 		h->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
  643: 		/*  seeds chosen so MASKED != RAW: a dropped `&` (raw returned as status) reds P0  */
  644: 		h->irq_status = 0x18;  h->irq_enable = 0x30;	/*  masked 0x10, raw 0x18  */
  645: 		h->fiq_status = 0x60;  h->fiq_enable = 0xc0;	/*  masked 0x40, raw 0x60  */
  646: 		fatal_calls = 0;	/*  this block counts announces from a clean baseline  */
  647: #define P_STATE_OK(tag) do { \
  648: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- irq_status preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->irq_status, 0x18); \
  649: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- irq_enable preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->irq_enable, 0x30); \
  650: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- fiq_status preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->fiq_status, 0x60); \
  651: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- fiq_enable preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->fiq_enable, 0xc0); } while (0)
  652: #define PN(suffix) (snprintf(nm, sizeof(nm), "%s: %s", dec[i].what, suffix), nm)
  653: 		/*  the legal reads FIRST, on the seeded state: the expressions, not a constant 0  */
  654: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  655: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  656: 		check_u("P0 IRQ_STATUS read = irq_status & irq_enable (0x10)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x10);
  657: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  658: 		check_u("P0 IRQ_RAW_STATUS read = irq_status (0x18)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x18);
  659: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  660: 		check_u("P0 FIQ_STATUS read = fiq_status & fiq_enable (0x40)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x40);
  661: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  662: 		check_u("P0 FIQ_RAW_STATUS read = fiq_status (0x60)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x60);
  663: 		check_u("P0 the legal reads announce nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
  664: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
  665: 			int a0 = irq_asserts, d0 = irq_deasserts, rv;
  666: 			f0 = fatal_calls;
  667: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);	/*  bits in BOTH enables: 0x70&0x30=0x30, 0x70&0xc0=0x40  */
  668: 			rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, dec[i].len, dec[i].wf, h);
  669: 			check_u(PN("handler returned 1 (declined, not failed)"), rv, 1);
  670: 			check_u(PN("announced exactly once"), fatal_calls - f0, 1);
  671: 			if (dec[i].wf == MEM_READ)
  672: 				check_u(PN("the declined read returns 0"), memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0);
  673: 			P_STATE_OK(PN("after #1"));
  674: 			check_u(PN("interrupt output untouched"), (irq_asserts - a0) + (irq_deasserts - d0), 0);
  675: 			f0 = fatal_calls;
  676: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
  677: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, dec[i].len, dec[i].wf, h);
  678: 			check_u(PN("#2 announced nothing more (latched)"), fatal_calls - f0, 0);
  679: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
  680: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, 1, dec[i].wf, h);
  681: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
  682: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, 2, dec[i].wf, h);
  683: 			check_u(PN("byte and halfword accesses: still latched"), fatal_calls - f0, 0);
  684: 			P_STATE_OK(PN("after #2, byte, halfword"));
  685: 		}
  686: 		/*  the latches are per SITE: five accesses, five announces, none shared  */
  687: 		check_u("P6 five sites announced five times in one instance", fatal_calls, 5);
  688: 		/*  a legal read after the declines still reads the seeded expressions  */
  689: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  690: 		check_u("P7 IRQ_STATUS read after the declines (0x10)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x10);
  691: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  692: 		check_u("P7 FIQ_RAW_STATUS read after the declines (0x60)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x60);
  693: 		/*  a LEGAL EnableClear write still works, next to the declined read of the same address  */
  694: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x10);
  695: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
  696: 		check_u("P8 legal EnableClear write: 0x30 & ~0x10", h->irq_enable, 0x20);
  697: 		check_u("P8 ... announced nothing", fatal_calls, 5);
  698: #undef PN
  699: #undef P_STATE_OK
  700: 		free(h);
  701: 		/*  P9: a FRESH instance whose FIRST access to a site is a BYTE write.  A decline that fires only
  702: 		    for len == 4 would let the byte fall through silently -- indistinguishable above, where the
  703: 		    word access had already latched the site.  Here it must announce.  */
  704: 		h = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
  705: 		arm_irqs(h);
  706: 		h->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
  707: 		h->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
  708: 		h->irq_status = 0x18;  h->irq_enable = 0x30;
  709: 		fatal_calls = 0;
  710: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 1, 0x70);
  711: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 1, MEM_WRITE, h);
  712: 		check_u("P9 a BYTE write as the first access is declined AND announced", fatal_calls, 1);
  713: 		check_u("P9 ... and stores nothing", h->irq_status, 0x18);
  714: 		free(h);
  715: 		fatal_calls = 0;
  716: 	}
  717: 
  718: 	/*  #545 pass 2 (L2 floor repair, measured by the Opus seat): the P-rows above pinned width and latch at ONE site
  719: 	    each, so a `len == 4` conjunct on any of the other four decline guards, a legal read that re-arms a latch, and
  720: 	    a decline guard widened to swallow a LEGAL narrow read all passed every row.  X1: every site at len 1 and 2 as
  721: 	    the FIRST access announces once (and the narrow declined read returns 0).  X2: legal narrow reads of the
  722: 	    five readable registers return the low lanes and announce nothing.  X3: legal reads of every sibling at every
  723: 	    width, legal enable writes, and reads of other registers re-arm NO latch.  X4/X5 (pass 2, codex): ZERO data
  724: 	    or a zero enable as the first access still declines AND announces; a legal zero Clear write does not.  */
  725: 	{
  726: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; const char *what; } s5[] = {
  727: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write IRQ_STATUS" }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write IRQ_RAW_STATUS" },
  728: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ, "X1 read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR" }, { FIQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write FIQ_STATUS" },
  729: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write FIQ_RAW_STATUS" } };
  730: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; uint64_t want; const char *what; } rd5[] = {
  731: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, 0x10, "IRQ_STATUS" }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, 0x18, "IRQ_RAW_STATUS" },
  732: 			{ FIQ_STATUS, 0x40, "FIQ_STATUS" }, { FIQ_RAW_STATUS, 0x60, "FIQ_RAW_STATUS" },
  733: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_SET, 0x30, "IRQ_ENABLE_SET" } };
  734: 		unsigned char b[8];
  735: 		char xn[160];
  736: 		int i, w, f0;
  737: 		struct footbridge_data *x;
  738: #define XSEED(x) do { arm_irqs(x); (x)->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert; (x)->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert; \
  739: 		(x)->irq_status = 0x18; (x)->irq_enable = 0x30; (x)->fiq_status = 0x60; (x)->fiq_enable = 0xc0; } while (0)
  740: 		for (w = 1; w <= 2; w++)
  741: 			for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
  742: 				x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSEED(x);
  743: 				fatal_calls = 0;
  744: 				memory_writemax64(cpu, b, w, 0x70);
  745: 				dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, w, s5[i].wf, x);
  746: 				snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "%s: len %d as the FIRST access announces once", s5[i].what, w);
  747: 				check_u(xn, fatal_calls, 1);
  748: 				if (s5[i].wf == MEM_READ) {	/*  the narrow declined read's VALUE (pass 2)  */
  749: 					snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X1 read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR: len %d declined read returns 0", w);
  750: 					check_u(xn, memory_readmax64(cpu, b, w), 0);
  751: 				}
  752: 				free(x);
  753: 			}
  754: 		x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSEED(x);
  755: 		fatal_calls = 0;
  756: 		for (w = 1; w <= 2; w++)
  757: 			for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
  758: 				memset(b, 0, sizeof(b));
  759: 				dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, rd5[i].addr, b, w, MEM_READ, x);
  760: 				snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X2 legal len %d read of %s = low lanes", w, rd5[i].what);
  761: 				check_u(xn, memory_readmax64(cpu, b, w), rd5[i].want & (w == 1 ? 0xff : 0xffff));
  762: 			}
  763: 		check_u("X2 the legal narrow reads announce nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
  764: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
  765: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
  766: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
  767: 		}
  768: 		check_u("X3 the five declines latch (5 announces)", fatal_calls, 5);
  769: 		f0 = fatal_calls;
  770: 		for (w = 1; w <= 4; w *= 2)
  771: 			for (i = 0; i < 5; i++)
  772: 				dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, rd5[i].addr, b, w, MEM_READ, x);
  773: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
  774: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
  775: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
  776: 		}
  777: 		check_u("X3 legal reads of every sibling re-arm NO latch", fatal_calls - f0, 0);
  778: 		/*  X3, widened (pass 2): legal WRITES to the four enable registers, and reads of registers outside the
  779: 		    five above (VENDOR_ID, TIMER_1_VALUE, the silent FIQEnableClear read), re-arm no latch either.  */
  780: 		f0 = fatal_calls;
  781: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0);
  782: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_SET, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
  783: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
  784: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_SET, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
  785: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
  786: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, VENDOR_ID, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
  787: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_VALUE, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
  788: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
  789: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
  790: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
  791: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
  792: 		}
  793: 		check_u("X3 legal ZERO-data enable writes and other-register reads re-arm NO latch", fatal_calls - f0, 0);
  794: 		free(x);
  795: 		/*  X4 (codex, pass 2): the FIRST access carries ZERO -- a zero-data write, or the +0x18c read with
  796: 		    IRQEnable 0.  A latch guard conditioned on the data (`&& idata`), a +0x18c guard conditioned on
  797: 		    irq_enable, or a direction test rewritten as `!idata`, lets such an access through without an
  798: 		    announce; each must announce once and change no field.  (A WRITE-site guard conditioned on an
  799: 		    enable -- X4 keeps the seeded enables there -- is X6's all-zero-state cells, not X4's.)  */
  800: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
  801: 			x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSEED(x);
  802: 			if (s5[i].wf == MEM_READ)
  803: 				x->irq_enable = 0;
  804: 			fatal_calls = 0;
  805: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0);
  806: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
  807: 			snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X4 %s: ZERO data/enable as the FIRST access announces once", s5[i].what + 3);
  808: 			check_u(xn, fatal_calls, 1);
  809: 			snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X4 %s: ZERO data/enable changes no field", s5[i].what + 3);
  810: 			check_u(xn, (x->irq_status == 0x18) + (x->irq_enable == (s5[i].wf == MEM_READ ? 0u : 0x30u))
  811: 			    + (x->fiq_status == 0x60) + (x->fiq_enable == 0xc0), 4);
  812: 			free(x);
  813: 		}
  814: 		/*  X5 (codex, pass 2): a LEGAL ZERO EnableClear write is a write, not a declined read -- a direction test
  815: 		    rewritten as `idata` sends it to the decline and announces.  */
  816: 		x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSEED(x);
  817: 		fatal_calls = 0;
  818: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0);
  819: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
  820: 		check_u("X5 a legal ZERO EnableClear write announces nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
  821: 		check_u("X5 ... and leaves IRQEnable at 0x30", x->irq_enable, 0x30);
  822: #undef XSEED
  823: 		free(x);
  824: 		fatal_calls = 0;
  825: 	}
  826: 
  827: 	/*
  828: 	 *  #545 CLASS CLOSURE (repair pass 2 -- two consecutive passes found breaches of ONE class, so the
  829: 	 *  whole space is enumerated from the handler's own inputs and closed in one block; carrier rule).
  830: 	 *
  831: 	 *  X6: EVERY DECLINED ACCESS -- site (5) x width (1, 2, 4) x data (zero, nonzero; writes only) x state
  832: 	 *      (seeded, all-zero, wide) x occurrence (first, repeated).  Each cell, as ONE row, requires: the handler
  833: 	 *      returns 1; the announce count moves by exactly 1 on the first access and 0 on the repeat; all four
  834: 	 *      register fields are unchanged; the interrupt output is untouched (no assert, no deassert); and a
  835: 	 *      declined read returns 0 over its whole width.
  836: 	 *  X7: EVERY LEGAL ACCESS RE-ARMS NO LATCH -- with all five sites latched: each enable write (Set/Clear,
  837: 	 *      IRQ/FIQ) with zero and nonzero data, and each legal read of the readable registers plus VENDOR_ID
  838: 	 *      and TIMER_1_VALUE at widths 1, 2 and 4; from the seeded, all-zero and wide states (so a read that
  839: 	 *      returns 0 is covered, and so is a field bit above byte 0).  After each, re-declining all five sites
  840: 	 *      must announce nothing.
  841: 	 *  X9 (below X8) is the other half of the legal side: every legal access with the latches still CLEAR.
  842: 	 *
  843: 	 *  CONTRACT (clause e): the class is ACCESS (declined, legal) x STATE (seeded, all-zero, wide) x LATCH
  844: 	 *  (clear, set) at these five sites -- declined x both latches in X6, legal x set in X7, legal x clear in
  845: 	 *  X9.  *** THIS SAID "the cells above are the whole class" UNTIL THE ADJUDICATION PASS OF 2026-09-23, AND
  846: 	 *  IT WAS MEASURED FALSE: X7 latched every site BEFORE its legal access, so LEGAL x ALL-ZERO x LATCH-CLEAR
  847: 	 *  -- the reset state -- had no row, and five one-conjunct edits to the DIRECTION test (`writeflag ==
  848: 	 *  MEM_READ && d->irq_enable` and its mirrors) passed all 333 rows here and all 37 guest rows
  849: 	 *  (regress/artifacts/fbd5/q2_20260923/Q2.log). ***  The DEPENDS-ON bound these rows use: the predicates
  850: 	 *  dominating each edited decline (case label, direction test, latch), and the lvalues read by BOTH
  851: 	 *  branches under the direction test -- the four interrupt fields and the five latches.  Not in the
  852: 	 *  class: the interrupt output after a LEGAL access except where X9 pins it, any other register's
  853: 	 *  semantics, WIDE DATA on a declined write (data stays 0 / 0x70), joint conjuncts over two fields
  854: 	 *  beyond the three states, and a latch guard conditioned on a field neither branch reads (measured F1:
  855: 	 *  `&& !timer_control[0]` passes every row here; the guest sites probe catches it only because its
  856: 	 *  prologue arms timer 1; table4_20260919).
  857: 	 */
  858: 	{
  859: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; const char *nm; } S5[] = {
  860: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "write IRQ_STATUS" }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "write IRQ_RAW_STATUS" },
  861: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ, "read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR" }, { FIQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "write FIQ_STATUS" },
  862: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "write FIQ_RAW_STATUS" } };
  863: 		/*  #545 adjudication (Fable 5.1, Q2 (e) cell): the byte seeds left bits 8-31 of every field unexercised,
  864: 		    so a decline store or a latch clearer keyed on them passed every row.  "wide" is X8's seeds.  */
  865: 		static const struct { uint32_t is, ie, fs, fe; const char *nm; } ST[3] = {

   37: 
   38: #include <stdio.h>
   39: #include <stdlib.h>
   40: #include <string.h>
