   54: 
   55: #define	DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_TICK_SHIFT	14
   56: #define	DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_LENGTH		0x400
   57: 
   58: #define	N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS		4
   59: 
   60: struct footbridge_data {
   61: 	struct interrupt irq;
   62: 
   63: 	struct pci_data *pcibus;
   64: 
   65: 	int		console_handle;
   66: 
   67: 	uint32_t	timer_load[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
   68: 	uint32_t	timer_value[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
   69: 	uint32_t	timer_control[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
   70: 
   71: 	struct interrupt timer_irq[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
   72: 	struct timer	*timer[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
   73: 	int		pending_timer_interrupts[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
   74: 	/*  #442: the per-timer bound on the counter above, one emulated second of
   75: 	    that timer's delivered rate, recomputed by reload_timer_value().  */
   76: 	int		pending_bound[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
   77: 
   78: 	int		irq_asserted;
   79: 
   80: 	uint32_t	irq_status;
   81: 	uint32_t	irq_enable;
   82: 
   83: 	uint32_t	fiq_status;
   84: 	uint32_t	fiq_enable;
   85: 
   86: 	/*  #545: one warn-once latch per undefined-direction access that used
   87: 	    to end the process (DC21285 7.3.29-7.3.33: "The Status and RawStatus
   88: 	    are read-only, and EnableClear and Soft are write-only").  Zeroed by
   89: 	    DEVINIT's memset and by the offline differentials' calloc.  */
   90: 	int		warned_irq_status_w;
   91: 	int		warned_irq_raw_w;
   92: 	int		warned_irq_clear_r;
   93: 	int		warned_fiq_status_w;
   94: 	int		warned_fiq_raw_w;
   95: };
   96: 
   97: 
   98: /*
   99:  *  #442: the counter above was incremented without a bound, so a guest that armed
  100:  *  a timer and never acked overflowed it -- and on overflow the int goes negative,
  101:  *  DEVICE_TICK's `> 0` is false, and THE EMULATED CLOCK DELIVERS NOTHING FOR 2^31
  102:  *  FURTHER TICKS.  Reproduced at rung 3 on an unmodified -E cats: two MMIO writes,
  103:  *  then 42.95 s to the wrap and a 40 s dead window, measured three times.
  104:  *
  105:  *  THE COMPARISON PRECEDES THE INCREMENT, and that is not a style choice.  An
  106:  *  increment-then-check form -- which is what the one in-tree precedent for this
  107:  *  (dev_luna88k.c:252) actually does -- reintroduces the UB it is meant to remove
  108:  *  if the counter ever reaches INT_MAX, because the ++ has already executed by the
  109:  *  time the check runs.  luna88k is safe only because its own reset keeps the
  110:  *  counter far below INT_MAX; the FORM is still the wrong one to copy.
  111:  *
  112:  *  Reset to 1 rather than 0, so one interrupt is still owed -- coalescing the debt
  113:  *  rather than discarding it.  MEASURED to matter: a reset-to-0 mutant survives the
  114:  *  rung-3 drain probe and two of the three offline rows; only the trough row
  115:  *  separates them (0 against 1).
  116:  *
  117:  *  NO MESSAGE FROM HERE.  These run in the SIGALRM handler and debugmsg() is
  118:  *  printf underneath, so copying luna88k's diagnostic would put four printf paths
  119:  *  inside a signal handler -- and timer.c:274-279 states that nothing in that file
  120:  *  prints from the handler.  (luna88k's own callback does, which is a defect there;
  121:  *  filed, not fixed here.)  If a diagnostic is ever wanted, latch a flag here and
  122:  *  report from DEVICE_TICK, which runs in the main loop.
  123:  */
  124: static void footbridge_pending(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr)
  125: {
  126: 	if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] >= d->pending_bound[nr])
  127: 		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] = 1;
  128: 	else
  129: 		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] ++;
  130: }
  131: 
  132: static void timer_tick0(struct timer *t, void *extra)
  133: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 0); }
  134: static void timer_tick1(struct timer *t, void *extra)
  135: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 1); }
  136: static void timer_tick2(struct timer *t, void *extra)
  137: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 2); }
  138: static void timer_tick3(struct timer *t, void *extra)
  139: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 3); }
  140: 
  141: 
  142: /*
  143:  *  #432: ONE interpretation of the load register, used by BOTH consumers.
  144:  *
  145:  *  THE CRASH.  DEVICE_TICK computed `random() % timer_load[i]`, and TIMER_x_LOAD accepts a
  146:  *  guest-written zero (`idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL`), so a guest could divide by zero and take the
  147:  *  HOST process down -- reproduced as SIGFPE, exit 136, by two seats independently.
  148:  *
  149:  *  THERE ARE TWO ROUTES TO IT, and only one is ours.  Route 1: a zero load makes the rate
  150:  *  +INF, which before #427/#428 meant interval 0.0 and a catch-up loop that never advanced,
  151:  *  so the machine froze before this function ran; the clamp and the bound opened that path.
  152:  *  ROUTE 2 PREDATES BOTH: TIMER_x_LOAD does not clear pending_timer_interrupts (TIMER_x_CONTROL
  153:  *  below does), so an ORDINARY LEGAL timer accrues a backlog and a later write of zero divides
  154:  *  -- measured on the pre-#427 core, it crashes there too.  DEVICE_TICK runs from machine_run(),
  155:  *  the main loop, not from the signal handler, so the CPU reaches the divide between signals.
  156:  *
  157:  *  WHY A HELPER RATHER THAN TWO GUARDS.  Three seats converged on this independently, and the
  158:  *  measuring seat REFUTED each single-site alternative: guarding only this function still
  159:  *  SIGFPEs by route 2 (and is the most dangerous option precisely because it passes the obvious
  160:  *  test); guarding only the modulo leaves the backlog growing 1048576 per signal, reaching
  161:  *  INT_MAX in ~31 s; and normalising at the guest write corrupts read-back -- TIMER_x_LOAD
  162:  *  returns the stored value, so the guest would read something it never wrote, which invents an
  163:  *  answer to a question the hardware documentation we lack would have to settle.
  164:  *
  165:  *  THE REGISTER STAYS RAW.  timer_load[] keeps exactly what the guest wrote, masked to 24 bits.
  166:  *  Only the DERIVED quantities are interpreted here, and they are model-internal.
  167:  *
  168:  *  ZERO MEANS FULL PERIOD (2^24), and it is a CHOICE UNDER A DOCUMENTED UNKNOWN, not a fact.
  169:  *
  170:  *  The 21285 datasheet was obtained for this round (_scratchpad/dc21285.pdf, Intel order
  171:  *  278115-001, Sept 1998).  SS 7.3.39 (p. 7-50) specifies TimerNLoad and its reset value without
  172:  *  saying what a count of zero does, and a search of the full extraction for "minimum count" /
  173:  *  "nonzero" / "reaches zero" turns up no equivalent of the 8254's Figure 22.  So the absence is
  174:  *  MEASURED, not assumed -- a stronger thing to say than the guess it replaces.
  175:  *
  176:  *  BUT "SILENT" OVERSTATES IT BY HALF, and the pass-2 review caught that in this round's favour.
  177:  *  SS 7.3.41 bit 6 says a FREE-RUNNING timer "will wrap to FFFFFFh and continue to decrement",
  178:  *  which together with SS 6.4's 24-bit down counter DETERMINES the free-run case: a load of zero
  179:  *  gives a period of exactly 2^24, i.e. the mapping below is datasheet-IMPLIED there -- and
  180:  *  free-running is bit 6 == 0, the reset mode.  It is genuinely unknown only for PERIODIC.
  181:  *
  182:  *  The choice therefore rests on failure asymmetry, and there are THREE candidate truths rather
  183:  *  than the two first written here.  Implement full-period when the truth is "disabled" and a
  184:  *  guest gets a few unwanted interrupts on a timer it explicitly enabled -- noisy, visible,
  185:  *  recoverable.  Implement "disabled" when the truth is full-period and the guest waits forever
  186:  *  on a tick that never comes -- silent, and it hangs at boot.  And the third, which the first
  187:  *  draft omitted: read mechanically, PERIODIC mode with a zero load reloads zero and interrupts
  188:  *  every prescaled clock -- a max-rate storm, the DANGEROUS branch rather than the silent one.
  189:  *  Full-period is the only choice that survives all three, so the argument was understating its
  190:  *  own robustness.
  191:  *
  192:  *  A SECOND SUPPORT WAS ALSO WITHDRAWN, for the same reason as the third and by a seat whose
  193:  *  answer sat unread for a day.  It said the 24-bit mask aliases "a legitimate guest write of
  194:  *  0x1000000 -- one full wrap -- to zero, so this mapping keeps the rate continuous across the
  195:  *  register's own truncation".  The datasheet closes it: SS 7.3.39 gives TimerNLoad as bits 23:0
  196:  *  R/W with "31:24 -- R Read only as 0", so bit 24 is not a truncated write at all, it is OUTSIDE
  197:  *  THE WRITABLE FIELD.  There is no wrap to be continuous across, and continuity beyond a
  198:  *  register's own width is a modelling preference dressed up as arithmetic.  Zero -> 2^24 stands
  199:  *  on the three-way argument above and on nothing else; what silicon does with a zero load
  200:  *  remains UNKNOWN, and SS 7.3.39 is silent on it.
  201:  *
  202:  *  A THIRD ARGUMENT WAS WITHDRAWN WHEN THE DATASHEET ARRIVED, and it is recorded because the
  203:  *  withdrawal matters more than the argument did.  An earlier draft of this comment said
  204:  *  "devinit resets every load to TIMER_MAX_VAL, so the model's own convention is full width".
  205:  *  The datasheet says TimerNLoad and TimerNControl BOTH reset to 0 (SS 7.3.39, SS 7.3.41), so
  206:  *  this model's reset values are themselves wrong -- that line was citing a model bug as
  207:  *  evidence.  The divergence is filed; it is not fixed here.
  208:  *
  209:  *  THE WIDTH FIX IS NOT SEPARABLE FROM THE ZERO FIX.  The old `int cycles` with `cycles <<= 8`
  210:  *  overflowed for exactly half the legal 24-bit range (first bad load 0x800000), yielding a
  211:  *  NEGATIVE frequency that the core's floor clamp silently turned into one tick per ~3.2 years
  212:  *  -- reachable with NO load write at all, since the reset load is TIMER_MAX_VAL and a single
  213:  *  write of ENABLE|FCLK_256 gets there.  And mapping zero to 2^24 while leaving `int` gives
  214:  *  2^24 << 8 == 2^32 == 0, which is +INF AGAIN: the obvious zero-guard RE-CREATES the bug it
  215:  *  removes.  Hence uint64_t, and multiplication rather than a signed shift.  Found independently
  216:  *  by three seats.
  217:  *
  218:  *  (8253 maps a zero divisor to "disabled" instead -- and a pass-2 seat caught that an earlier
  219:  *  version of this parenthetical argued the point BACKWARDS.  It said the 8253's 16-bit counter
  220:  *  "has no equivalent of the 24-bit aliasing above", but writing 0x10000 to a 16-bit latch aliases
  221:  *  to zero in exactly the same way -- which is precisely WHY the Intel 8254 datasheet defines a
  222:  *  programmed count of 0 as 2^16 (231164-005, p. 17).  So the aliasing argument REINFORCES this
  223:  *  mapping rather than distinguishing it, and dev_8253.c's "disabled" is the one that looks wrong
  224:  *  against its own hardware.  Filed as its own item; not changed here.)
  225:  */
  226: /*
  227:  *  with_prescaler distinguishes the two consumers, and it is NOT a convenience flag -- getting
  228:  *  it wrong is a defect the measuring seat caught in its own first proposal.  The prescaler
  229:  *  slows the RATE; it does not widen the COUNTER.  Feeding the prescaled period to the modulo
  230:  *  put values up to 0x7fffca11 into a 24-bit register that the guest can read back -- measured,
  231:  *  19833 of 20000 ticks out of range.  So: the rate consumer prescales, the counter consumer
  232:  *  does not, and both get their never-zero span from this one function.
  233:  */
  234: static uint64_t footbridge_effective_cycles(uint32_t load, uint32_t control,
  235: 	int with_prescaler)
  236: {
  237: 	uint64_t cycles = load;
  238: 
  239: 	if (cycles == 0)
  240: 		cycles = (uint64_t)TIMER_MAX_VAL + 1;	/*  0x1000000 == 2^24  */
  241: 
  242: 	if (!with_prescaler)
  243: 		return cycles;
  244: 
  245: 	/*  The prescaler scales the PERIOD.  It must not be a signed shift, and the order
  246: 	    matters: TIMER_EXTERNAL is FCLK_16|FCLK_256, so test the pair first or 0x0C reads
  247: 	    as a bare FCLK_16 by luck of the `else if`.  */
  248: 	if ((control & (TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256))
  249: 	    == (TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256)) {
  250: 		/*  TIMER_EXTERNAL (0x0C): a LEGAL encoding, and this is now DATASHEET-BACKED
  251: 		    rather than inferred from the header.  The 21285 datasheet obtained for this
  252: 		    round gives TimerNControl bits 3:2 as 00=fclk_in, 01=fclk_in/16,
  253: 		    10=fclk_in/256, 11=External event (SS 7.3.41, p. 7-51; SS 6.4, p. 6-11 lists
  254: 		    the same four).  It used to reach fatal()+exit(1) below, so a CONFORMING guest
  255: 		    selecting the external clock source killed the host -- the same defect class as
  256: 		    the divide and arguably likelier, being a documented mode rather than a
  257: 		    malformed value.  The RATE an external source would give depends on a pin this
  258: 		    model has no signal for (irq_in_l), so it is genuinely not derivable here and
  259: 		    is NOT invented: the unprescaled period keeps the timer sane and the emulator
  260: 		    alive.  Modelling the external event properly is filed.  */
  261: 		;
  262: 	} else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_16)
  263: 		cycles *= 16;
  264: 	else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_256)
  265: 		cycles *= 256;
  266: 
  267: 	return cycles;
  268: }
  269: 
  270: 
  271: static void reload_timer_value(struct cpu *cpu, struct footbridge_data *d,
  272: 	int timer_nr)
  273: {
  274: 	double freq = (double)cpu->machine->emulated_hz;
  275: 
  276: 	freq /= (double) footbridge_effective_cycles(d->timer_load[timer_nr],
  277: 	    d->timer_control[timer_nr], 1);
  278: 
  279: 	d->timer_value[timer_nr] = d->timer_load[timer_nr];
  280: 
  281: 	/*
  282: 	 *  #442: one emulated second of THIS timer's delivered rate, clamped.
  283: 	 *
  284: 	 *  The ceiling is DERIVED, not chosen: TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP ticks per timer
  285: 	 *  per signal at TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY signals per second is the most the
  286: 	 *  timer core can ever deliver to one timer in one wall second, so a cap
  287: 	 *  at that value can never throttle a legitimately fast timer.
  288: 	 *
  289: 	 *  IT IS ALSO NOT OPTIONAL, which a purely rate-relative bound hides.
  290: 	 *  MEASURED: with `-I 2147483647` and load 1 the requested rate is
  291: 	 *  2147483647.0, so (int)freq is INT_MAX and a bound of "the timer's own
  292: 	 *  rate" never fires -- the overflow returns, reachable through a
  293: 	 *  documented command-line flag.  Deleting these two lines is the
  294: 	 *  smallest edit that still passes the rung-3 probe.
  295: 	 *
  296: 	 *  Floor 1.  *** ITS ORIGINAL JUSTIFICATION WAS MEASURED FALSE and is
  297: 	 *  corrected here rather than quietly dropped: *** the comment claimed a
  298: 	 *  0.0116 Hz timer "would otherwise bound at 0 and the counter would be
  299: 	 *  pinned there".  With the floor PRESENT that case already reads peak 1,
  300: 	 *  and with the floor DELETED the harness output is BYTE-IDENTICAL -- a
  301: 	 *  bound of 0 and a bound of 1 both pin at 1.  The floor prevents nothing
  302: 	 *  today; it is kept as a guard against a future bound expression that
  303: 	 *  could go negative, and that is the honest reason.
  304: 	 *
  305: 	 *  The comparisons are negated, but NOT for the reason first written.
  306: 	 *  timer_clamp_freq() puts its FLOOR first deliberately (timer.c:80-82:
  307: 	 *  "the FLOOR is tested FIRST so a NaN lands SLOW rather than fast"), and
  308: 	 *  this tests the CEILING first -- the inverse.  Harmless, because NaN is
  309: 	 *  UNREACHABLE here: emulated_hz is an int (machine.h:191) and
  310: 	 *  footbridge_effective_cycles() is >= 1 by construction, so this is
  311: 	 *  finite / finite-nonzero.  The claim to "match timer_clamp_freq's idiom"
  312: 	 *  was false in the one respect that idiom is about.
  313: 	 *
  314: 	 *  Set before timer_add()/timer_update_frequency() below.  *** THE REASON
  315: 	 *  FIRST GIVEN HERE -- that a SIGALRM landing between them would find a
  316: 	 *  zero bound -- IS NOT LOAD-BEARING, measured. ***  On the FIRST arm the
  317: 	 *  timer is not in first_timer until timer_add() links it, and timer.c
  318: 	 *  publishes fully-initialised (next stored before first_timer); on later
  319: 	 *  arms the OLD bound is still a valid bound.  A mutant moving this store
  320: 	 *  below the switch is a ZERO-BYTE object delta and passes every row.
  321: 	 *
  322: 	 *  Kept in this order because it reads correctly and costs nothing, not
  323: 	 *  because it closes a window.  Note also that this and the DEVINIT seed
  324: 	 *  are REDUNDANT WITH EACH OTHER -- either alone removes the zero-bound
  325: 	 *  state -- and neither comment used to mention the other doing the work.
  326: 	 */
  327: 	{
  328: 		double bound = freq;
  329: 		if (!(bound <= (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY))
  330: 			bound = (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY;
  331: 		if (!(bound >= 1.0))
  332: 			bound = 1.0;
  333: 		d->pending_bound[timer_nr] = (int) bound;
  334: 	}
  335: 
  336: 	/*  printf("%i: %i -> %f Hz\n", timer_nr,
  337: 	    d->timer_load[timer_nr], freq);  */
  338: 
  339: 	if (d->timer[timer_nr] == NULL) {
  340: 		switch (timer_nr) {
  341: 		case 0:	d->timer[0] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick0, d); break;
  342: 		case 1:	d->timer[1] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick1, d); break;
  343: 		case 2:	d->timer[2] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick2, d); break;
  344: 		case 3:	d->timer[3] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick3, d); break;
  345: 		}
  346: 	} else {
  347: 		timer_update_frequency(d->timer[timer_nr], freq);
  348: 	}
  349: }
  350: 
  351: 
  352: /*
  353:  *  The 4 footbridge timers should decrease and cause interrupts. Periodic
  354:  *  interrupts restart as soon as they are acknowledged, non-periodic
  355:  *  interrupts need to be "reloaded" to restart.
  356:  *
  357:  *  TODO: Hm. I thought I had solved this, but it didn't quite work.
  358:  *        This needs to be re-checked against documentation, sometime.
  359:  */
  360: DEVICE_TICK(footbridge)
  361: {
  362: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
  363: 	int i;
  364: 
  365: 	for (i=0; i<N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
  366: 		if (d->timer_control[i] & TIMER_ENABLE) {
  367: 			if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[i] > 0) {
  368: 				/*  #432: the same never-zero span, WITHOUT the
  369: 				    prescaler -- this is a 24-bit counter, and the
  370: 				    prescaler slows the rate rather than widening
  371: 				    it.  */
  372: 				d->timer_value[i] = random() %
  373: 				    footbridge_effective_cycles(d->timer_load[i],
  374: 				    d->timer_control[i], 0);
  375: 				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->timer_irq[i]);
  376: 			}
  377: 		}
  378: 	}
  379: }
  380: 
  381: 
  382: /*
  383:  *  footbridge_interrupt_assert():
  384:  */
  385: void footbridge_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
  386: {
  387: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
  388: 	d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;
  389: 
  390: 	// Patch from NetBSD:
  391: 	// "fix hang interrupt issue. Always assert the irq's even if we have
  392: 	//  already asserted the global irq for the device. prevents hangs with
  393: 	//  cats."
  394: 	if ((d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) /* && !d->irq_asserted */) {
  395: 		d->irq_asserted = 1;
  396: 		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
  397: 	}
  398: }
  399: 
  400: 
  401: /*
  402:  *  footbridge_interrupt_deassert():
  403:  */
  404: void footbridge_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
  405: {
  406: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
  407: 	d->irq_status &= ~interrupt->line;
  408: 
  409: 	if (!(d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) && d->irq_asserted) {
  410: 		d->irq_asserted = 0;
  411: 		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
  412: 	}
  413: }
  414: 
  415: 
  416: /*
  417:  *  Reading the byte at 0x79000000 is a quicker way to figure out which ISA
  418:  *  interrupt has occurred (and acknowledging it at the same time), than
  419:  *  dealing with the legacy 0x20/0xa0 ISA ports.
  420:  */
  421: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_isa)
  422: {
  423: 	/*  struct footbridge_data *d = extra;  */
  424: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
  425: 	int x;
  426: 
  427: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
  428: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
  429: 		fatal("[ footbridge_isa: WARNING/TODO: write! idata = 0x%016" PRIx64" ]\n", idata);
  430: 	}
  431: 
  432: 	x = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.last_int;
  433: 	if (x < 8)
  434: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic1->irq_base + x;
  435: 	else
  436: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic2->irq_base + x - 8;
  437: 
  438: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  439: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
  440: 
  441: 	return 1;
  442: }
  443: 
  444: 
  445: /*
  446:  *  Reset pin at ISA port 0x338, at least in the NetWinder:
  447:  *
  448:  *  TODO: NOT WORKING YET!
  449:  */
  450: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_reset)
  451: {
  452: 	uint64_t idata = 0;
  453: 
  454: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
  455: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
  456: 		if (idata & 0x40) {
  457: 			debug("[ footbridge_reset: GP16: Halting. ]\n");
  458: 			/*  #220: (Codex/Fable) cpu->running=0 already halts cleanly;
  459: 			    the col-0 exit(1) was a debug hack that kills the host.  */
  460: 			cpu->running = 0;
  461: 		}
  462: 	}
  463: 
  464: 	return 1;
  465: }
  466: 
  467: 
  468: /*
  469:  *  The Footbridge PCI configuration space is implemented as a direct memory
  470:  *  space (i.e. not one port for addr and one port for data). This function
  471:  *  translates that into bus_pci calls.
  472:  */
  473: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_pci)
  474: {
  475: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
  476: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
  477: 	int bus, dev, func, reg;
  478: 
  479: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
  480: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len|MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN);
  481: 
  482: 	/*  Decompose the (direct) address into its components:  */
  483: 	bus_pci_decompose_1(relative_addr, &bus, &dev, &func, &reg);
  484: 	bus_pci_setaddr(cpu, d->pcibus, bus, dev, func, reg);
  485: 
  486: 	if (bus == 255) {
  487: 		fatal("[ footbridge DEBUG ERROR: bus 255 unlikely,"
  488: 		    " pc (might not be updated) = 0x%08x ]\n", (int)cpu->pc);
  489: 		/*  #220: (Codex/Fable) don't exit() the host on a guest PCI
  490: 		    access to the (unlikely) bus 255; treat it as handled.  */
  491: 		return 1;
  492: 	}
  493: 
  494: 	debug("[ footbridge pci: %s bus %i, device %i, function %i, register "
  495: 	    "%i ]\n", writeflag == MEM_READ? "read from" : "write to", bus,
  496: 	    dev, func, reg);
  497: 
  498: 	bus_pci_data_access(cpu, d->pcibus, writeflag == MEM_READ?
  499: 	    &odata : &idata, len, writeflag);
  500: 
  501: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  502: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len|MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN, odata);
  503: 
  504: 	return 1;
  505: }
  506: 
  507: 
  508: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)
  509: {
  510: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
  511: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
  512: 	int timer_nr = 0;
  513: 
  514: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
  515: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
  516: 
  517: 	if (relative_addr >= TIMER_1_LOAD && relative_addr <= TIMER_4_CLEAR) {
  518: 		timer_nr = (relative_addr >> 5) & (N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS - 1);
  519: 		relative_addr &= ~0x060;
  520: 	}
  521: 
  522: 	switch (relative_addr) {
  523: 
  524: 	case VENDOR_ID:
  525: 		odata = 0x1011;  /*  DC21285_VENDOR_ID  */
  526: 		break;
  527: 
  528: 	case DEVICE_ID:
  529: 		odata = 0x1065;  /*  DC21285_DEVICE_ID  */
  530: 		break;
  531: 
  532: 	case 0x04:
  533: 	case 0x0c:
  534: 	case 0x10:
  535: 	case 0x14:
  536: 	case 0x18:
  537: 		/*  TODO. Written to by Linux.  */
  538: 		break;
  539: 
  540: 	case REVISION:
  541: 		odata = 3;  /*  footbridge revision number  */
  542: 		break;
  543: 
  544: 	case PCI_ADDRESS_EXTENSION:
  545: 		/*  TODO: Written to by Linux.  */
  546: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
  547: 			fatal("[ footbridge: TODO: write to PCI_ADDRESS_"
  548: 			    "EXTENSION: 0x%llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
  549: 		break;
  550: 
  551: 	case SA_CONTROL:
  552: 		/*  Read by Linux:  */
  553: 		odata = PCI_CENTRAL_FUNCTION;
  554: 		break;
  555: 
  556: 	case UART_DATA:
  557: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
  558: 			console_putchar(d->console_handle, idata);
  559: 		break;
  560: 
  561: 	case UART_RX_STAT:
  562: 		/*  TODO  */
  563: 		odata = 0;
  564: 		break;
  565: 
  566: 	case UART_FLAGS:
  567: 		odata = UART_TX_EMPTY;
  568: 		break;
  569: 
  570: 	case IRQ_STATUS:
  571: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  572: 			odata = d->irq_status & d->irq_enable;
  573: 		else if (!d->warned_irq_status_w) {
  574: 			/*  #545: read-only register (7.3.29); the write is
  575: 			    declined -- no register changes, only this site's
  576: 			    warn-once latch is set -- and announced once.  */
  577: 			d->warned_irq_status_w = 1;
  578: 			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to irq status? "
  579: 			    "(once) ]\n");
  580: 		}
  581: 		break;
  582: 
  583: 	case IRQ_RAW_STATUS:
  584: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  585: 			odata = d->irq_status;
  586: 		else if (!d->warned_irq_raw_w) {
  587: 			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.30); declined, announced once.  */
  588: 			d->warned_irq_raw_w = 1;
  589: 			fatal("[ footbridge write to irq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
  590: 		}
  591: 		break;
  592: 
  593: 	case IRQ_ENABLE_SET:
  594: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
  595: 			d->irq_enable |= idata;
  596: 			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
  597: 				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
  598: 			else
  599: 				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
  600: 		} else {
  601: 			/*  #539: a read of 188h is documented -- IRQEnable is
  602: 			    "read-only ... can only be changed by writing to
  603: 			    the EnableSet and EnableClear registers", reset 0
  604: 			    (DC21285 Table 7-10, 7.3.31).  The value below was
  605: 			    always right; the exit(1) that followed it is gone.  */
  606: 			odata = d->irq_enable;
  607: 		}
  608: 		break;
  609: 
  610: 	case IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
  611: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
  612: 			d->irq_enable &= ~idata;
  613: 			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
  614: 				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
  615: 			else
  616: 				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
  617: 		} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {
  618: 			/*  #545: IRQEnableClear is write-only (7.3.33) and Table
  619: 			    7-10 names no register at 18Ch for reads, so the read
  620: 			    is declined with 0 -- the IRQEnable value that used to
  621: 			    be computed here is 188h's answer, not 18Ch's.  */
  622: 			d->warned_irq_clear_r = 1;
  623: 			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge read from "
  624: 			    "ENABLE CLEAR? (once) ]\n");
  625: 		}
  626: 		break;
  627: 
  628: 	case FIQ_STATUS:
  629: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  630: 			odata = d->fiq_status & d->fiq_enable;
  631: 		else if (!d->warned_fiq_status_w) {
  632: 			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.29); declined, announced once.  */
  633: 			d->warned_fiq_status_w = 1;
  634: 			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to fiq status? "
  635: 			    "(once) ]\n");
  636: 		}
  637: 		break;
  638: 
  639: 	case FIQ_RAW_STATUS:
  640: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  641: 			odata = d->fiq_status;
  642: 		else if (!d->warned_fiq_raw_w) {
  643: 			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.30); declined, announced once.  */
  644: 			d->warned_fiq_raw_w = 1;
  645: 			fatal("[ footbridge write to fiq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
  646: 		}
  647: 		break;
  648: 
  649: 	case FIQ_ENABLE_SET:
  650: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
  651: 			d->fiq_enable |= idata;
  652: 		break;
  653: 
  654: 	case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
  655: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
  656: 			d->fiq_enable &= ~idata;
  657: 		break;
  658: 
  659: 	case TIMER_1_LOAD:
  660: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  661: 			odata = d->timer_load[timer_nr];
  662: 		else {
  663: 			d->timer_load[timer_nr] = idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
  664: 			reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
  665: 			/*  debug("[ footbridge: timer %i (1-based), "
  666: 			    "value %i ]\n", timer_nr + 1,
  667: 			    (int)d->timer_value[timer_nr]);  */
  668: 			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
  669: 		}
  670: 		break;
  671: 
  672: 	case TIMER_1_VALUE:
  673: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  674: 			odata = d->timer_value[timer_nr];
  675: 		else
  676: 			d->timer_value[timer_nr] = idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
  677: 		break;
  678: 
  679: 	case TIMER_1_CONTROL:
  680: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  681: 			odata = d->timer_control[timer_nr];
  682: 		else {
  683: 			d->timer_control[timer_nr] = idata;
  684: 			/*  #432: both prescaler bits set is TIMER_EXTERNAL
  685: 			    (0x0C, dc21285reg.h:364) -- a LEGAL named encoding,
  686: 			    and it used to reach fatal()+exit(1) right here, so a
  687: 			    guest selecting the external clock source KILLED THE
  688: 			    HOST.  Arguably likelier than the zero load, since it
  689: 			    is a documented mode rather than a malformed value.
  690: 			    The rate an external clock would actually give is
  691: 			    UNKNOWN and is NOT invented: footbridge_effective_cycles()
  692: 			    now gives the encoding a defined, survivable meaning
  693: 			    (the unprescaled period), and the real semantics are
  694: 			    filed for a round that has a datasheet.  */
  695: 			if (idata & TIMER_ENABLE) {
  696: 				reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
  697: 			} else {
  698: 				d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] = 0;
  699: 			}
  700: 			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
  701: 		}
  702: 		break;
  703: 
  704: 	case TIMER_1_CLEAR:
  705: 		/*  #474: a READ must not perform the clear.  This case carried no
  706: 		    writeflag test at all, while TIMER_1_CONTROL immediately above it
  707: 		    does -- so a guest READ reloaded the counter, decremented the
  708: 		    pending count and DEASSERTED the interrupt.  A guest that merely
  709: 		    read the register silently acknowledged a pending timer interrupt;
  710: 		    the guest-visible consequence is a LOST interrupt, not a bad value.
  711: 
  712: 		    FOUR addresses, not one: the fold above normalises 0x30C/0x32C/
  713: 		    0x34C/0x36C onto this single case.
  714: 
  715: 		    GUARD THE BODY, KEEP THE break.  An early return here would skip
  716: 		    the function's common tail, which writes odata back on a read, and
  717: 		    would hand a guest an untouched buffer -- a defect this code does
  718: 		    not currently have.  */
  719: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
  720: 			if (d->timer_control[timer_nr] & TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC) {
  721: 				reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
  722: 			}
  723: 
  724: 			if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] > 0) {
  725: 				d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] --;
  726: 			}
  727: 
  728: 			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
  729: 		}
  730: 		break;
  731: 
  732: 	case SDRAM_BA_MASK:
  733: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
  734: 			fatal("[ footbridge read from sdram_ba_mask ]\n");
  735: 			exit(1);
  736: 		} else {
  737: 			switch (idata) {
  738: 			case SDRAM_MASK_256KB:
  739: 			case SDRAM_MASK_512KB:
  740: 			case SDRAM_MASK_1MB:
  741: 			case SDRAM_MASK_2MB:
  742: 			case SDRAM_MASK_4MB:
  743: 			case SDRAM_MASK_8MB:
  744: 			case SDRAM_MASK_16MB:
  745: 			case SDRAM_MASK_32MB:
  746: 			case SDRAM_MASK_64MB:
  747: 			case SDRAM_MASK_128MB:
  748: 			case SDRAM_MASK_256MB:
  749: 				break;
  750: 			default:
  751: 				fatal("[ footbridge write to sdram_ba_mask "
  752: 				    "%#llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
  753: 				break;
  754: 			}
  755: 		}
  756: 		break;
  757: 	case SDRAM_BA_OFFSET:
  758: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
  759: 			fatal("[ footbridge read from sdram_ba_offset ]\n");
  760: 			exit(1);
  761: 		} else {
  762: 			if (idata != 0)
  763: 				fatal("[ footbridge write to sdram_ba_offset "
  764: 				    "%#llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
  765: 		}
  766: 		break;
  767: 
  768: 	default:if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
  769: 			fatal("[ footbridge: read from 0x%x ]\n",
  770: 			    (int)relative_addr);
  771: 		} else {
  772: 			fatal("[ footbridge: write to 0x%x: 0x%llx ]\n",
  773: 			    (int)relative_addr, (long long)idata);
  774: 		}
  775: 	}
  776: 
  777: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  778: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
  779: 
  780: 	return 1;
  781: }
  782: 
  783: 
  784: DEVINIT(footbridge)
  785: {
  786: 	struct footbridge_data *d;
  787: 	char irq_path[300], irq_path_isa[400];
  788: 	uint64_t pci_addr = 0x7b000000;
  789: 	int i;
  790: 
  791: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct footbridge_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct footbridge_data)));
  792: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
  793: 
  794: 	/*
  795: 	 *  #442: the memset leaves pending_bound[] at 0, and a bound of 0 would pin
  796: 	 *  the counter at 1 rather than bounding it.  That is UNREACHABLE today --
  797: 	 *  reload_timer_value() sets the bound, and it is also the only thing that
  798: 	 *  ever creates a struct timer, so no callback can fire before a bound
  799: 	 *  exists.  Initialised here anyway, because that is a two-step argument
  800: 	 *  about a signal handler and a later edit adding a timer elsewhere would
  801: 	 *  silently break it.
  802: 	 *
  803: 	 *  *** THE SEED IS 1, NOT THE CEILING, AND THE FIRST VERSION HAD THIS
  804: 	 *  BACKWARDS. ***  It called the ceiling "the safe default: it can never
  805: 	 *  throttle, only bound."  The ceiling is 68,157,440 -- at cats' 762.94 Hz
  806: 	 *  that is TWENTY-FIVE HOURS of un-acked backlog, i.e. pre-fix behaviour
  807: 	 *  for any run shorter than a day.  Measured: a mutant seeding exactly
  808: 	 *  that value reads 3051 on the drain probe, indistinguishable from
  809: 	 *  pre-fix.  If the scenario this seed exists for ever happened, it would
  810: 	 *  hand that timer the LARGEST bound the fix permits.  1 can only
  811: 	 *  under-deliver, which is the right direction for a value that should
  812: 	 *  never be used.
  813: 	 */
  814: 	for (i = 0; i < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++)
  815: 		d->pending_bound[i] = 1;
  816: 
  817: 	/*  Connect to the CPU which this footbridge will interrupt:  */
  818: 	INTERRUPT_CONNECT(devinit->interrupt_path, d->irq);
  819: 
  820: 	/*  DC21285 register access:  */
  821: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, devinit->name,
  822: 	    devinit->addr, DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_LENGTH,
  823: 	    dev_footbridge_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
  824: 
  825: 	/*  ISA interrupt status/acknowledgement:  */
  826: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "footbridge_isa",
  827: 	    0x79000000, 8, dev_footbridge_isa_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
  828: 
  829: 	/*  The "fcom" console:  */
  830: 	d->console_handle = console_start_slave(devinit->machine, "fcom", 0);
  831: 
  832: 	/*  Register 32 footbridge interrupts:  */
  833: 	snprintf(irq_path, sizeof(irq_path), "%s.footbridge",
  834: 	    devinit->interrupt_path);
  835: 	for (i=0; i<32; i++) {
  836: 		struct interrupt interrupt_template;
  837: 		char tmpstr[600];
  838: 
  839: 		memset(&interrupt_template, 0, sizeof(interrupt_template));
  840: 		interrupt_template.line = (uint32_t)1 << i;
  841: 		snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "%s.%i", irq_path, i);
  842: 		interrupt_template.name = tmpstr;
  843: 
  844: 		interrupt_template.extra = d;
  845: 		interrupt_template.interrupt_assert =
  846: 		    footbridge_interrupt_assert;
  847: 		interrupt_template.interrupt_deassert =
  848: 		    footbridge_interrupt_deassert;
  849: 		interrupt_handler_register(&interrupt_template);
  850: 
  851: 		/*  Connect locally to some interrupts:  */
  852: 		if (i>=IRQ_TIMER_1 && i<=IRQ_TIMER_4)
  853: 			INTERRUPT_CONNECT(tmpstr, d->timer_irq[i-IRQ_TIMER_1]);
  854: 	}
  855: 
  856: 	switch (devinit->machine->machine_type) {
  857: 	case MACHINE_CATS:
  858: 		snprintf(irq_path_isa, sizeof(irq_path_isa), "%s.10", irq_path);
  859: 		break;
  860: 	case MACHINE_NETWINDER:
  861: 		snprintf(irq_path_isa, sizeof(irq_path_isa), "%s.11", irq_path);
  862: 		break;
  863: 	default:fatal("footbridge unimpl machine type\n");
  864: 		exit(1);
  865: 	}
  866: 
  867: 	/*  A PCI bus:  */
  868: 	d->pcibus = bus_pci_init(
  869: 	    devinit->machine,
  870: 	    irq_path,
  871: 	    0x7c000000,		/*  PCI device io offset  */
  872: 	    0x80000000,		/*  PCI device mem offset  */
  873: 	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI port base  */
  874: 	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI mem base  */
  875: 	    irq_path,		/*  PCI irq base  */
  876: 	    0x7c000000,		/*  ISA port base  */
  877: 	    0x80000000,		/*  ISA mem base  */
  878: 	    irq_path_isa);	/*  ISA port base  */
  879: 
  880: 	/*  ... with some default devices for known machine types:  */
  881: 	switch (devinit->machine->machine_type) {
  882: 	case MACHINE_CATS:
  883: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
  884: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 7, 0, "ali_m1543");
  885: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
  886: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 10, 0, "dec21143");
  887: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
  888: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 16, 0, "ali_m5229");
  889: 		break;
  890: 	case MACHINE_NETWINDER:
  891: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
  892: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 11, 0, "symphony_83c553");
  893: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
  894: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 11, 1, "symphony_82c105");
  895: 		memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory,
  896: 		    "footbridge_reset", 0x7c000338, 1,
  897: 		    dev_footbridge_reset_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
  898: 		break;
  899: 	default:fatal("footbridge: unimplemented machine type.\n");
  900: 		exit(1);
  901: 	}
  902: 
  903: 	/*  PCI configuration space:  */
  904: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory,
  905: 	    "footbridge_pci", pci_addr, 0x1000000,
  906: 	    dev_footbridge_pci_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
  907: 
  908: 	/*  Timer ticks:  */
  909: 	for (i=0; i<N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
  910: 		d->timer_control[i] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC;
  911: 		d->timer_load[i] = TIMER_MAX_VAL;
  912: 	}
  913: 
  914: 	machine_add_tickfunction(devinit->machine,
  915: 	    dev_footbridge_tick, d, DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_TICK_SHIFT);
  916: 
  917: 	devinit->return_ptr = d->pcibus;
  918: 	return 1;
  919: }
  920:

  360: DEVICE_TICK(footbridge)
  361: {
  362: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
  363: 	int i;
  364: 
  365: 	for (i=0; i<N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
  366: 		if (d->timer_control[i] & TIMER_ENABLE) {
  367: 			if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[i] > 0) {
  368: 				/*  #432: the same never-zero span, WITHOUT the
  369: 				    prescaler -- this is a 24-bit counter, and the
  370: 				    prescaler slows the rate rather than widening
  371: 				    it.  */
  372: 				d->timer_value[i] = random() %
  373: 				    footbridge_effective_cycles(d->timer_load[i],
  374: 				    d->timer_control[i], 0);
  375: 				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->timer_irq[i]);
  376: 			}
  377: 		}
  378: 	}
  379: }
  380: 
  381: 
  382: /*
  383:  *  footbridge_interrupt_assert():
  384:  */
  385: void footbridge_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
  386: {
  387: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
  388: 	d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;
  389: 
  390: 	// Patch from NetBSD:
  391: 	// "fix hang interrupt issue. Always assert the irq's even if we have
  392: 	//  already asserted the global irq for the device. prevents hangs with
  393: 	//  cats."
  394: 	if ((d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) /* && !d->irq_asserted */) {
  395: 		d->irq_asserted = 1;
  396: 		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
  397: 	}
  398: }
  399: 
  400: 
  401: /*
  402:  *  footbridge_interrupt_deassert():
  403:  */
  404: void footbridge_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
  405: {
  406: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
  407: 	d->irq_status &= ~interrupt->line;
  408: 
  409: 	if (!(d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) && d->irq_asserted) {
  410: 		d->irq_asserted = 0;
  411: 		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
  412: 	}
  413: }
  414: 
  415: 
  416: /*
  417:  *  Reading the byte at 0x79000000 is a quicker way to figure out which ISA
  418:  *  interrupt has occurred (and acknowledging it at the same time), than
  419:  *  dealing with the legacy 0x20/0xa0 ISA ports.
  420:  */
  421: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_isa)
  422: {
  423: 	/*  struct footbridge_data *d = extra;  */
  424: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
  425: 	int x;
  426: 
  427: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
  428: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
  429: 		fatal("[ footbridge_isa: WARNING/TODO: write! idata = 0x%016" PRIx64" ]\n", idata);
  430: 	}
  431: 
  432: 	x = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.last_int;
  433: 	if (x < 8)
  434: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic1->irq_base + x;
  435: 	else
  436: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic2->irq_base + x - 8;
  437: 
  438: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  439: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
  440: 
  441: 	return 1;
  442: }
  443: 
  444: 
  445: /*
  446:  *  Reset pin at ISA port 0x338, at least in the NetWinder:
  447:  *
  448:  *  TODO: NOT WORKING YET!
  449:  */
  450: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_reset)
  451: {
  452: 	uint64_t idata = 0;
  453: 
  454: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
  455: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
  456: 		if (idata & 0x40) {
  457: 			debug("[ footbridge_reset: GP16: Halting. ]\n");
  458: 			/*  #220: (Codex/Fable) cpu->running=0 already halts cleanly;
  459: 			    the col-0 exit(1) was a debug hack that kills the host.  */
  460: 			cpu->running = 0;
  461: 		}
  462: 	}
  463: 
  464: 	return 1;
  465: }
  466: 
  467: 
  468: /*
  469:  *  The Footbridge PCI configuration space is implemented as a direct memory
  470:  *  space (i.e. not one port for addr and one port for data). This function
  471:  *  translates that into bus_pci calls.
  472:  */
  473: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_pci)
  474: {
  475: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
  476: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
  477: 	int bus, dev, func, reg;
  478: 
  479: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
  480: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len|MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN);
  481: 
  482: 	/*  Decompose the (direct) address into its components:  */
  775: 	}
  776: 
  777: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  778: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
  779: 
  780: 	return 1;
  781: }

  784: DEVINIT(footbridge)
  785: {
  786: 	struct footbridge_data *d;
  787: 	char irq_path[300], irq_path_isa[400];
  788: 	uint64_t pci_addr = 0x7b000000;
  789: 	int i;
  790: 
  791: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct footbridge_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct footbridge_data)));
  792: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
  793: 
  794: 	/*
  795: 	 *  #442: the memset leaves pending_bound[] at 0, and a bound of 0 would pin
  796: 	 *  the counter at 1 rather than bounding it.  That is UNREACHABLE today --
  797: 	 *  reload_timer_value() sets the bound, and it is also the only thing that
  798: 	 *  ever creates a struct timer, so no callback can fire before a bound
  799: 	 *  exists.  Initialised here anyway, because that is a two-step argument
  800: 	 *  about a signal handler and a later edit adding a timer elsewhere would
  801: 	 *  silently break it.
  802: 	 *
  803: 	 *  *** THE SEED IS 1, NOT THE CEILING, AND THE FIRST VERSION HAD THIS
  804: 	 *  BACKWARDS. ***  It called the ceiling "the safe default: it can never
  805: 	 *  throttle, only bound."  The ceiling is 68,157,440 -- at cats' 762.94 Hz
  806: 	 *  that is TWENTY-FIVE HOURS of un-acked backlog, i.e. pre-fix behaviour
  807: 	 *  for any run shorter than a day.  Measured: a mutant seeding exactly
  808: 	 *  that value reads 3051 on the drain probe, indistinguishable from
  809: 	 *  pre-fix.  If the scenario this seed exists for ever happened, it would
  810: 	 *  hand that timer the LARGEST bound the fix permits.  1 can only
  811: 	 *  under-deliver, which is the right direction for a value that should
  812: 	 *  never be used.
  813: 	 */
  814: 	for (i = 0; i < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++)
  815: 		d->pending_bound[i] = 1;
  816: 
  817: 	/*  Connect to the CPU which this footbridge will interrupt:  */
  818: 	INTERRUPT_CONNECT(devinit->interrupt_path, d->irq);
  819: 
  820: 	/*  DC21285 register access:  */
  821: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, devinit->name,
  822: 	    devinit->addr, DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_LENGTH,
  823: 	    dev_footbridge_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
  824: 
  825: 	/*  ISA interrupt status/acknowledgement:  */
  826: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "footbridge_isa",
  827: 	    0x79000000, 8, dev_footbridge_isa_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
  828: 
  829: 	/*  The "fcom" console:  */
  830: 	d->console_handle = console_start_slave(devinit->machine, "fcom", 0);
  831: 
  832: 	/*  Register 32 footbridge interrupts:  */
  833: 	snprintf(irq_path, sizeof(irq_path), "%s.footbridge",
  834: 	    devinit->interrupt_path);
  835: 	for (i=0; i<32; i++) {
  836: 		struct interrupt interrupt_template;
  837: 		char tmpstr[600];
  838: 
  839: 		memset(&interrupt_template, 0, sizeof(interrupt_template));
  840: 		interrupt_template.line = (uint32_t)1 << i;
  841: 		snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "%s.%i", irq_path, i);
  842: 		interrupt_template.name = tmpstr;
  843: 
  844: 		interrupt_template.extra = d;
  845: 		interrupt_template.interrupt_assert =
  846: 		    footbridge_interrupt_assert;
  847: 		interrupt_template.interrupt_deassert =
  848: 		    footbridge_interrupt_deassert;
  849: 		interrupt_handler_register(&interrupt_template);
  850: 
  851: 		/*  Connect locally to some interrupts:  */
  852: 		if (i>=IRQ_TIMER_1 && i<=IRQ_TIMER_4)
  853: 			INTERRUPT_CONNECT(tmpstr, d->timer_irq[i-IRQ_TIMER_1]);
  854: 	}
  855: 
  856: 	switch (devinit->machine->machine_type) {
  857: 	case MACHINE_CATS:
  858: 		snprintf(irq_path_isa, sizeof(irq_path_isa), "%s.10", irq_path);
  859: 		break;
  860: 	case MACHINE_NETWINDER:
  861: 		snprintf(irq_path_isa, sizeof(irq_path_isa), "%s.11", irq_path);
  862: 		break;
  863: 	default:fatal("footbridge unimpl machine type\n");
  864: 		exit(1);
  865: 	}
  866: 
  867: 	/*  A PCI bus:  */
  868: 	d->pcibus = bus_pci_init(
  869: 	    devinit->machine,
  870: 	    irq_path,
  871: 	    0x7c000000,		/*  PCI device io offset  */
  872: 	    0x80000000,		/*  PCI device mem offset  */
  873: 	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI port base  */
  874: 	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI mem base  */
  875: 	    irq_path,		/*  PCI irq base  */
  876: 	    0x7c000000,		/*  ISA port base  */
  877: 	    0x80000000,		/*  ISA mem base  */
  878: 	    irq_path_isa);	/*  ISA port base  */
  879: 
  880: 	/*  ... with some default devices for known machine types:  */
  881: 	switch (devinit->machine->machine_type) {
  882: 	case MACHINE_CATS:
  883: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
  884: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 7, 0, "ali_m1543");
  885: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
  886: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 10, 0, "dec21143");
  887: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
  888: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 16, 0, "ali_m5229");
  889: 		break;
  890: 	case MACHINE_NETWINDER:
  891: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
  892: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 11, 0, "symphony_83c553");
  893: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
  894: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 11, 1, "symphony_82c105");
  895: 		memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory,
  896: 		    "footbridge_reset", 0x7c000338, 1,
  897: 		    dev_footbridge_reset_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
  898: 		break;
  899: 	default:fatal("footbridge: unimplemented machine type.\n");

    1: /*
    2:  *  Offline differential for the SHIPPED footbridge timer -- src/devices/dev_footbridge.c.
    3:  *
    4:  *  WHY THIS EXISTS.  #432 fixed a guest-reachable HOST CRASH: `random() % timer_load[i]` with
    5:  *  a guest-writable zero load is an integer division by zero, reproduced independently by two
    6:  *  seats as SIGFPE (exit 136).  A crash fix with no detector is this project's fifth vacuity
    7:  *  class, and here the detector had to be offline for a reason stronger than convenience:
    8:  *  THERE IS NO NETWINDER OR CATS RIG ANYWHERE IN regress/ -- nothing in this tree boots a
    9:  *  machine that has a footbridge.  So this is not the cheapest detector, it is the ONLY
   10:  *  possible one.
   11:  *
   12:  *  IT IS THE REPOSITORY FILE.  This driver #includes the shipped dev_footbridge.c, exactly as
   13:  *  diff_sh4_tmu.c #includes dev_sh4.c -- an identical header set, and the precedent that made
   14:  *  the feasibility question answerable at all.  Nothing is transcribed.
   15:  *
   16:  *  SEVEN STUBS, and `-Wl,--gc-sections` is LOAD-BEARING rather than cosmetic: without it the
   17:  *  link needs eleven more symbols (bus_pci_*, interrupt_*, console_start_slave,
   18:  *  machine_add_tickfunction, memory_device_register).  The trade-off, stated so it is not
   19:  *  mistaken for coverage: gc-sections drops DEVINIT, so this file cannot test devinit.
   20:  *
   21:  *  THE TWO STUBS THAT ARE INSTRUMENTS, not scaffolding: timer_add() and
   22:  *  timer_update_frequency() RECORD the frequency the device asked for.  That is what lets a
   23:  *  row assert the rate semantics directly, instead of inferring them from a boot that cannot
   24:  *  happen here.
   25:  *
   26:  *  EXPECTED VALUES ARE ABSOLUTES DERIVED FROM THE 24-BIT WIDTH, never re-read from the header
   27:  *  symbol the code itself uses.  R4's review established that a row which takes its expected
   28:  *  value from the code under test is green for ANY value of it (filed as `constblind`), so
   29:  *  16777216 is written out here on purpose.
   30:  */

   56: static int timer_add_calls, timer_update_calls;
   57: struct timer;
   58: struct timer *timer_add(double freq, void (*f)(struct timer *, void *), void *extra)
   59: {
   60: 	(void) f; (void) extra;

   72: uint64_t memory_readmax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len)
   73: {
   74: 	uint64_t x = 0; int i;
   75: 	(void) cpu;
   76: 	/*  Little-endian, which is what the rigs are; the device only ever reads back what
   77: 	    this driver wrote, so the convention only has to be self-consistent.  */
   78: 	for (i = 0; i < len; i++)
   79: 		x |= (uint64_t) buf[i] << (8*i);
   80: 	return x;
   81: }
   82: void memory_writemax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len, uint64_t data)
   83: {
   84: 	int i;
   85: 	(void) cpu;
   86: 	for (i = 0; i < len; i++)
   87: 		buf[i] = (data >> (8*i)) & 255;
   88: }
   89: void console_putchar(int handle, int ch) { (void) handle; (void) ch; }
   90: 
   91: /*  The device asserts an interrupt on every delivered tick, and INTERRUPT_ASSERT calls
   92:     through a function pointer in the struct.  A calloc'd struct leaves that NULL, so the
   93:     first tick SIGSEGVs -- which looks exactly like the crash under test and would have made
   94:     this whole file a liar.  Install counting no-ops instead.  */
   95: static int irq_asserts, irq_deasserts;
   96: static void noop_assert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; irq_asserts++; }
   97: static void noop_deassert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; irq_deasserts++; }

  102: static void arm_irqs(struct footbridge_data *d)
  103: {
  104: 	int i;
  105: 	for (i = 0; i < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
  106: 		d->timer_irq[i].interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
  107: 		d->timer_irq[i].interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
  108: 	}

  111: /* --------------------------------------------------------------- harness --- */
  112: 
  113: static int rows, fails;
  114: static void check(const char *name, const char *got, const char *want)
  115: {
  116: 	rows++;
  117: 	if (strcmp(got, want) == 0)
  118: 		printf("  ok    %-54s %s\n", name, got);
  119: 	else {
  120: 		fails++;
  121: 		printf("  FAIL  %-54s\n          got  %s\n          want %s\n",
  122: 		    name, got, want);
  123: 	}
  124: }
  125: static void check_u(const char *name, uint64_t got, uint64_t want)
  126: {
  127: 	char g[32], w[32];
  128: 	snprintf(g, sizeof(g), "%" PRIu64, got);
  129: 	snprintf(w, sizeof(w), "%" PRIu64, want);
  130: 	check(name, g, w);
  131: }

  133: /*  The helper is static inside the shipped file, which is exactly why this driver includes
  134:     the file rather than linking it.  */
  135: int main(void)
  136: {
  137: 	struct footbridge_data *d;
  138: 	struct cpu *cpu;
  139: 	struct machine *machine;
  140: 
  141: 	/*  LINE-BUFFERED ON PURPOSE.  gate_offline.sh redirects this binary's stdout to a file,
  142: 	    which makes it FULLY buffered -- so when a mutant re-introduces the crash, the log is
  143: 	    0 BYTES and the operator gets a red row with nothing in it.  Measured: line-buffered,
  144: 	    the same run leaves 943 bytes naming A1/B2/D1 as FAIL before it dies.  The gate's
  145: 	    greps fail either way, so this is legibility rather than detection -- but it is this
  146: 	    project's own recorded stdout-buffering trap, in a new place.  */
  147: 	setvbuf(stdout, NULL, _IOLBF, 0);
  148: 
  149: 	cpu = calloc(1, sizeof(struct cpu));
  150: 	machine = calloc(1, sizeof(struct machine));
  151: 	d = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
  152: 	cpu->machine = machine;
  153: 	machine->emulated_hz = 100000000;	/*  100 MHz, an ordinary rate  */
  154: 
  155: 	{
  156: 		/*
  157: 		 *  SELFCHECK -- CAN THIS FILE'S COMPARATOR STILL FAIL?
  158: 		 *
  159: 		 *  Feed check() deliberate mismatches and require the failure counter to move,
  160: 		 *  then un-record them so the rows are free.  If check() has been stubbed,
  161: 		 *  neutered, or had its comparison edited away, EVERY other row in this file is
  162: 		 *  silently inert and the gate cannot tell -- measured: 118 rows across five
  163: 		 *  detectors stayed green under exactly that stub, row counts, identity rows and
  164: 		 *  verdict tokens all intact.
  165: 		 *
  166: 		 *  TWO MISMATCHES, ONE ALPHABETIC AND ONE DIGIT-LEADING, and the second is not
  167: 		 *  padding.  The first version fed only "a" vs "b", and a pass-2 seat defeated it
  168: 		 *  with one line -- `isdigit(got[0]) || strcmp(...) == 0` -- because EVERY REAL ROW
  169: 		 *  IN THIS FILE COMPARES NUMBERS.  118 rows went inert, this sentinel stayed blind,
  170: 		 *  and four of the five gate-2 self-mutants stayed green too.  A sentinel whose
  171: 		 *  input does not resemble the rows it vouches for is a sentinel with a whitelist.
  172: 		 *
  173: 		 *  THE HEALTHY PATH PRINTS AN ok-SHAPED LINE, DELIBERATELY.  The first version
  174: 		 *  printed nothing on success and a FAIL line only on failure, which broke two
  175: 		 *  things at once: there was no stable string for gate 2 to grep, so DELETING THIS
  176: 		 *  WHOLE BLOCK FROM ALL FIVE FILES left the gate at PASS with the same 220 checks
  177: 		 *  and zero red rows -- and the FAIL text it printed on the HEALTHY path polluted
  178: 		 *  the gate's kill attribution, so a self-mutant pinned to a row id like "fail"
  179: 		 *  matched this line and reported OK for a mutant nothing had caught.
  180: 		 *
  181: 		 *  `>` not `== +1`: a comparator that double-counts compares perfectly well, and
  182: 		 *  the exact form printed "no longer compares" at it.  Direction was safe, the
  183: 		 *  diagnosis was wrong.
  184: 		 *
  185: 		 *  This does NOT prove any row is correct.  It proves the comparator still fails on
  186: 		 *  inputs shaped like the ones the rows use.  A comparator that compares
  187: 		 *  SELECTIVELY -- or that special-cases this row by name -- is still invisible to
  188: 		 *  it, and only a census would see that.  Liveness sentinel, nothing more.
  189: 		 *
  190: 		 *  THE PROBE ROWS ARE NAMED @@SELFCHECK@@ SO THEY CANNOT BE MISTAKEN FOR A ROW.
  191: 		 *  They are deliberate mismatches, so check() prints FAIL for them on the HEALTHY
  192: 		 *  path -- and a pass-2 seat measured that a self-mutant pinned to a row id like
  193: 		 *  "fail" matched one of those lines and reported OK for a mutant nothing caught.
  194: 		 *  The obvious cure is worse than the disease: suppressing the print INSIDE check()
  195: 		 *  would need a name test in the comparator, which is precisely the whitelist the
  196: 		 *  same seat showed defeats this sentinel (`strncmp(name,"SELFCHECK",9)`).  So the
  197: 		 *  comparator is left alone and the probe rows carry a token no real row would use;
  198: 		 *  selfmutant.py filters it, and gate 2 greps the SUMMARY line below for presence.
  199: 		 */
  200: 		int selfcheck_f = fails, selfcheck_r = rows, selfcheck_bad = 0;
  201: 
  202: 		check("@@SELFCHECK@@/str", "a", "b");
  203: 		if (fails <= selfcheck_f)
  204: 			selfcheck_bad++;
  205: 		fails = selfcheck_f; rows = selfcheck_r;
  206: 
  207: 		check("@@SELFCHECK@@/num", "1", "2");
  208: 		if (fails <= selfcheck_f)
  209: 			selfcheck_bad++;
  210: 		fails = selfcheck_f; rows = selfcheck_r;
  211: 
  212: 		if (selfcheck_bad) {
  213: 			printf("  FAIL  SELFCHECK the comparator no longer compares (%d of 2 "
  214: 			    "mismatches went unnoticed) -- every row in this file is inert\n",
  215: 			    selfcheck_bad);
  216: 			fails = selfcheck_f + 1;
  217: 			rows = selfcheck_r;
  218: 		} else {
  219: 			printf("  ok    SELFCHECK the comparator can still fail       "
  220: 			    "str+num\n");
  221: 		}
  222: 	}
  223: 
  224: 	printf("--- A. the load interpretation: zero is a FULL PERIOD, never a divisor of 0 ---\n");
  225: 	/*  16777216 == 2^24, written as an absolute rather than read from TIMER_MAX_VAL: a row
  226: 	    that takes its expected value from the code under test cannot notice the code
  227: 	    changing (the `constblind` finding).  */
  228: 	check_u("A1 load 0 becomes the full 24-bit span",
  229: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0, 0, 0), 16777216);
  230: 	check_u("A2 an ordinary load is untouched",
  231: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(1000, 0, 0), 1000);
  232: 	check_u("A3 the maximum load is untouched",
  233: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0xffffff, 0, 0), 16777215);
  234: 
  235: 	printf("--- B. the prescaler is 64-bit MULTIPLICATION, not a signed shift ---\n");
  236: 	/*  THE ROW THAT CATCHES THE DEFECT THE OBVIOUS FIX RE-CREATES.  The old code used
  237: 	    `int cycles` with `cycles <<= 8`; three seats found independently that this
  238: 	    overflows for exactly half the legal range, and that mapping zero to 2^24 while
  239: 	    leaving `int` gives 2^24 << 8 == 2^32 == 0 -> +INF again.  B1 and B2 are the two
  240: 	    halves of that: the reset load prescaled, and the zero load prescaled.  */
  241: 	check_u("B1 reset load x256 does not overflow",
  242: 	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0xffffff, TIMER_FCLK_256, 1),
  243: 	    (uint64_t) 16777215 * 256);
  244: 	check_u("B2 zero load x256 does not wrap to zero",

  433: 	    is a LOST interrupt, not a corrupted value.
  434: 
  435: 	    FOUR addresses, not one: the timer fold normalises 0x30C/0x32C/0x34C/0x36C onto
  436: 	    this single case.
  437: 
  438: 	    These rows drive the ACCESS HANDLER rather than poking struct fields, for the
  439: 	    reason this file already records above: a struct-poking row cannot see a
  440: 	    handler-level defect at all.  */
  441: 	{
  442: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(*h));
  443: 		unsigned char buf[4];
  444: 		uint64_t back;
  445: 
  446: 		arm_irqs(h);
  447: 
  448: 		/*  CONTROL first: a WRITE must decrement.  Without it a green defect row
  449: 		    could equally mean the handler never ran.  */
  450: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  451: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  452: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
  453: 		check_u("[LIVE] a WRITE of TIMER_1_CLEAR decrements pending",
  454: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 1);
  455: 
  456: 		/*  THE DEFECT: a READ must leave the pending count alone.  */
  457: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  458: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  459: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  460: 		/*  THE GUARD WRAPS THREE EFFECTS AND THE ROWS ABOVE OBSERVE TWO.
  461: 		    reload_timer_value and the pending decrement are both pinned;
  462: 		    INTERRUPT_DEASSERT was not.  A panel seat named the mutant that
  463: 		    exploits the gap -- hoist the deassert OUT of the guard so a READ
  464: 		    still deasserts:
  465: 
  466: 		        if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) { reload; pending--; }
  467: 		        INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
  468: 
  469: 		    Every other row passes -- the deassert touches neither the pending
  470: 		    count nor timer_value -- while the guest READ once again
  471: 		    acknowledges a pending interrupt.  **That is #474's headline
  472: 		    symptom, reintroduced**: the round's own record calls the
  473: 		    consequence a LOST interrupt, and a lost interrupt IS a spurious
  474: 		    deassert.
  475: 
  476: 		    The counter was already armed by noop_deassert and NEVER READ.
  477: 		    A counter nothing asserts on is a row that does not exist.  */
  478: 		/*  THE ACCEPT COMPANION, and the row below is HALF A ROW without it.
  479: 		    A row that wants ZERO can only fail UPWARD: delete
  480: 		    INTERRUPT_DEASSERT from the guarded body outright and the READ
  481: 		    row stays green, because zero deasserts is exactly what it
  482: 		    asserts.  Measured by a panel seat against the version that
  483: 		    shipped this row without its pair.
  484: 
  485: 		    This is the de-escalation clause's accept side in miniature:
  486: 		    the fix made a channel CONDITIONAL, so the detector owes a row
  487: 		    proving the channel still fires on the legal path.  */
  488: 		irq_deasserts = 0;
  489: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  490: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  491: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
  492: 		check_u("[LIVE] a WRITE DOES deassert the irq", irq_deasserts, 1);
  493: 
  494: 		irq_deasserts = 0;
  495: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  496: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  497: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  498: 		check_u("a READ must NOT deassert the irq", irq_deasserts, 0);
  499: 
  500: 		check_u("a READ of TIMER_1_CLEAR must NOT clear pending",
  501: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
  502: 
  503: 		/*  ENABLE.  A measuring seat named this the worst of five one-token
  504: 		    escapes: widen the guard with `|| (timer_control & TIMER_ENABLE)`
  505: 		    and it sweeps the whole table, because every other row here uses
  506: 		    control = 0 or PERIODIC and never TIMER_ENABLE.
  507: 
  508: 		    It is the worst because of WHICH state it re-opens: a DISABLED
  509: 		    timer can never hold a pending interrupt, so the mutant re-opens
  510: 		    the defect on precisely the state this round exists for.  */
  511: 		h->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
  512: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  513: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  514: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  515: 		check_u("an ENABLED timer READ does not clear",
  516: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
  517: 		h->timer_control[0] = 0;
  518: 
  519: 		/*  WIDTH.  `&& len == 4` sweeps every row above -- they all use a
  520: 		    four-byte access.  The same shape as the wdc detector's own width
  521: 		    hole, found the same day in a different file.  */
  522: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  523: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  524: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 2, MEM_READ, h);
  525: 		check_u("a 2-byte READ does not clear either",
  526: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
  527: 
  528: 		/*  *** THE ANCHOR ROWS, and the premise they correct is mine. ***
  529: 		    This round's record said 'every row exercises timer 1'.  It does
  530: 		    not: TIMER_1_CLEAR is 0x30C and (0x30C >> 5) & 3 == 0, so every
  531: 		    row above is an INDEX-0 row.  That is exactly why `timer_nr = 0`
  532: 		    and a widened fold mask each score a clean sweep -- the file
  533: 		    claims FOUR addresses and carried no row distinguishing any of
  534: 		    them.
  535: 
  536: 		    0x32C selects index 1 and 0x36C index 3.  Asserting the NEIGHBOUR
  537: 		    is untouched is what makes these ANCHORED rather than merely
  538: 		    distinct: a re-map that collides on one probed cell still reds.
  539: 		    */
  540: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 5;
  541: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[1] = 2;
  542: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  543: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_2_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
  544: 		check_u("0x32C decrements timer index 1",
  545: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[1], 1);
  546: 		check_u("...and leaves index 0 untouched",
  547: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 5);
  548: 
  549: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[3] = 2;
  550: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  551: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_4_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
  552: 		check_u("0x36C decrements timer index 3",
  553: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[3], 1);
  554: 		check_u("...and index 0 is still untouched",
  555: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 5);
  556: 
  557: 		/*  A READ AT A NON-ZERO INDEX, and it is the row the anchor pair
  558: 		    above could not be: those drive WRITES, so a guard widened by
  559: 		    `|| timer_nr != 0` passes them for the ordinary reason and
  560: 		    nothing reds.  MEASURED: that mutant escaped the 32-row table
  561: 		    until this row existed.
  562: 
  563: 		    The general shape is worth naming: an ANCHOR row proves WHICH
  564: 		    cell an access lands on, and a GUARD row proves the access is
  565: 		    refused.  A mutant that widens the guard per-index needs both
  566: 		    properties in ONE row, and neither existing row had them.  */
  567: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[1] = 2;
  568: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  569: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_2_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  570: 		check_u("a READ at index 1 does not clear",
  571: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[1], 2);
  572: 
  573: 		/*  THE COMMON TAIL, and it is the row the fix's own comment needed.
  574: 		    That comment says the `break` is deliberate because an early
  575: 		    `return` would skip
  576: 		        if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  577: 		                memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
  578: 		    and hand a guest an untouched buffer.  Nothing tested that: buf was
  579: 		    memset to 0xff and never read, so `break` -> `return 1` passed both
  580: 		    rows.  A stated rationale with no row behind it is a claim, not a
  581: 		    property.  Same access as the row above -- the question is what that
  582: 		    call LEFT IN THE BUFFER, not what it did to the device.
  583: 
  584: 		    Zero is the tail writing an untouched odata, which is also what the
  585: 		    pre-fix code returned; the datasheet specifies no read value for a
  586: 		    write-only register, so this pins DETERMINISM, not architecture.  */
  587: 		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
  588: 		check_u("a READ still runs the common tail", back, 0);
  589: 
  590: 		/*  PERIODIC READ.  The rows above use control = 0, so the periodic arm
  591: 		    of the guarded body never runs and a guard widened to admit a
  592: 		    periodic read still passes them.  reload_timer_value() assigns
  593: 		    timer_value = timer_load, so a value deliberately UNEQUAL to load
  594: 		    makes the reload observable with no clock and no tick.
  595: 
  596: 		    This is the clause-(d) row for this fix: it keeps the guard and
  597: 		    nulls its repair on the one path the other rows cannot reach.  */
  598: 		h->timer_control[0] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC;
  599: 		h->timer_load[0]    = 0x1234;
  600: 		h->timer_value[0]   = 0x9999;	/*  deliberately != load  */
  601: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
  602: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
  603: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  604: 		check_u("a PERIODIC read must not reload the counter",
  605: 		    h->timer_value[0], 0x9999);
  606: 		check_u("a PERIODIC read must not clear pending either",
  607: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
  608: 
  609: 		free(h);
  610: 	}
  611: 
  612: 	/*
  613: 	 *  #545 P-ROWS -- the five undefined-direction accesses that used to end the process are
  614: 	 *  DECLINED with a latched announce.  These rows pin what no stepped guest can (the guest
  615: 	 *  rows live in footbridge_sites_probe.py, arm S): PRESERVATION under a NONZERO status, the
  616: 	 *  read EXPRESSIONS, the widths, and the latch -- fatal() is a counter here, so the announce
  617: 	 *  is counted, never read (the misname check is the guest rows' job).
  618: 	 *
  619: 	 *  SEEDED BY DIRECT FIELD WRITE, NEVER THROUGH +0x188/+0x18c.  A bare calloc'd instance has
  620: 	 *  NULL d->irq callbacks and the enable arms call INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq), so this block
  621: 	 *  installs counting no-ops FIRST; the seeds are still written directly, so that no enable-
  622: 	 *  arm assert is mixed into the interrupt-output counts the rows below compare.
  623: 	 *  Seeds (enumerated, pass 2): 0x18/0x30/0x60/0xc0 -- every field-for-field swap in either
  624: 	 *  status expression yields a DIFFERENT value (0x50 collided: 0x50 & 0x30 == 0x18 & 0x30).
  625: 	 *  fiq_status has no writer in the product; seeding it pins the +0x280/+0x284 expressions
  626: 	 *  against a value the product never produces -- a NARROWING of that gap, not its closure.
  627: 	 */
  628: 	{
  629: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
  630: 		unsigned char buf[8];
  631: 		char nm[96], nm2[128];
  632: 		int f0, i;
  633: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; int len; const char *what; } dec[] = {
  634: 			{ IRQ_STATUS,       MEM_WRITE, 4, "P1 write IRQ_STATUS"       },
  635: 			{ IRQ_RAW_STATUS,   MEM_WRITE, 4, "P2 write IRQ_RAW_STATUS"   },
  636: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ,  4, "P3 read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR"  },
  637: 			{ FIQ_STATUS,       MEM_WRITE, 4, "P4 write FIQ_STATUS"       },
  638: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS,   MEM_WRITE, 4, "P5 write FIQ_RAW_STATUS"   },
  639: 		};

  641: 		h->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
  642: 		h->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
  643: 		/*  seeds chosen so MASKED != RAW: a dropped `&` (raw returned as status) reds P0  */
  644: 		h->irq_status = 0x18;  h->irq_enable = 0x30;	/*  masked 0x10, raw 0x18  */
  645: 		h->fiq_status = 0x60;  h->fiq_enable = 0xc0;	/*  masked 0x40, raw 0x60  */
  646: 		fatal_calls = 0;	/*  this block counts announces from a clean baseline  */
  647: #define P_STATE_OK(tag) do { \
  648: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- irq_status preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->irq_status, 0x18); \
  649: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- irq_enable preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->irq_enable, 0x30); \
  650: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- fiq_status preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->fiq_status, 0x60); \
  651: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- fiq_enable preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->fiq_enable, 0xc0); } while (0)
  652: #define PN(suffix) (snprintf(nm, sizeof(nm), "%s: %s", dec[i].what, suffix), nm)
  653: 		/*  the legal reads FIRST, on the seeded state: the expressions, not a constant 0  */
  654: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
  655: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  656: 		check_u("P0 IRQ_STATUS read = irq_status & irq_enable (0x10)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x10);
  657: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  658: 		check_u("P0 IRQ_RAW_STATUS read = irq_status (0x18)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x18);
  659: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  660: 		check_u("P0 FIQ_STATUS read = fiq_status & fiq_enable (0x40)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x40);
  661: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
  662: 		check_u("P0 FIQ_RAW_STATUS read = fiq_status (0x60)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x60);
  663: 		check_u("P0 the legal reads announce nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
  664: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
  665: 			int a0 = irq_asserts, d0 = irq_deasserts, rv;

  664: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {

  718: 	/*  #545 pass 2 (L2 floor repair, measured by the Opus seat): the P-rows above pinned width and latch at ONE site

  721: 	    the FIRST access announces once (and the narrow declined read returns 0).  X2: legal narrow reads of the
  722: 	    five readable registers return the low lanes and announce nothing.  X3: legal reads of every sibling at every

  555: #  trade-off, stated so it is not mistaken for coverage: it drops DEVINIT, so this file cannot
  556: #  test devinit.  -D_DEFAULT_SOURCE is required for random() under -std=c99.
  557: #  MEASURED against seven mutants in copies -- all red, control passes: the zero guard removed
  558: #  (dies BY SIGNAL 8, the crash itself), the prescaler back to a signed shift, the tick site
  559: #  prescaling, TIMER_EXTERNAL falling through as a bare x16, and the three that PASS 2 found

  561: #  the tick counter replaced by a constant, and normalize-at-write (the design this round
  562: #  REJECTED).  An eighth is EQUIVALENT and deliberately not counted: on a uint64_t, `<<= 4` and

def reds(path, remap=False):
    return {rename.get(n,n) if remap else n for l in path.read_text().splitlines() if l.startswith('  FAIL  ') and '@@SELFCHECK@@' not in l for n in [l[8:].strip()]}
