1. **Q1 — premise audit.**

   **(a) The predicates are identified correctly; the claimed class closure is false.** Walking the frozen handler:

   - `0x180`, `0x184`, `0x280`, `0x284`: matching case → direction is not `MEM_READ` → own latch is clear.
   - `0x18c`: matching case → direction is not `MEM_WRITE` → own latch is clear.

   The producer uses `MEM_READ=0` and `MEM_WRITE=1`. I also inspected the preceding timer-address normalization and enumerated its mapping over the registered `0x400`-byte region. The preimages of these cases are exactly `{0x180}`, `{0x184}`, `{0x18c}`, `{0x280}`, `{0x284}`. There is no additional controlling predicate missing from your list.

   However, identifying the dependencies does not establish that three field tuples adequately partition their values. Furthermore, X7 observes only announcement counts after the legal access; it does not check the returned value, preservation of fields, or return code. See [X7’s assertion](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2r/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:930). Q2 gives an escape using only the expressly included direction predicate and own latch.

   **(b) X9’s eight write expectations are correct.** Each entry below gives the resulting targeted enable and callback. `A` means exactly one ASSERT; `D` exactly one DEASSERT; `—` neither.

   | Write | Seeded | All-zero | Wide |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | IRQ Set `0` | `0x30 / A` | `0 / D` | `0x00010030 / A` |
   | IRQ Set `0x70` | `0x70 / A` | `0x70 / D` | `0x00010070 / A` |
   | IRQ Clear `0` | `0x30 / A` | `0 / D` | `0x00010030 / A` |
   | IRQ Clear `0x70` | `0 / D` | `0 / D` | `0x00010000 / A` |
   | FIQ Set `0` | `0xc0 / —` | `0 / —` | `0x000200c0 / —` |
   | FIQ Set `0x70` | `0xf0 / —` | `0x70 / —` | `0x000200f0 / —` |
   | FIQ Clear `0` | `0xc0 / —` | `0 / —` | `0x000200c0 / —` |
   | FIQ Clear `0x70` | `0x80 / —` | `0 / —` | `0x00020080 / —` |

   In IRQ row order, the interrupt-expression results are:

   - Seeded: `{0x10, 0x10, 0x10, 0}`.
   - All-zero: `{0, 0, 0, 0}`.
   - Wide: `{0x10010, 0x10010, 0x10010, 0x10000}`.

   The handler leaves the other three fields untouched. X9 asserts these outcomes correctly.

   **(c) Yes, each wide field has bits above byte zero.** Enumerating the actual set bits:

   | Field | Set bits |
   |---|---|
   | `irq_status` | `{3, 4, 16, 31}` |
   | `irq_enable` | `{4, 5, 16}` |
   | `fiq_status` | `{5, 6, 17, 30}` |
   | `fiq_enable` | `{6, 7, 17}` |

   The four wide status-read results are:

   | Register | Calculation/result |
   |---|---|
   | IRQ_STATUS | `0x80010018 & 0x00010030 = 0x00010010` |
   | IRQ_RAW_STATUS | `0x80010018` |
   | FIQ_STATUS | `0x40020060 & 0x000200c0 = 0x00020040` |
   | FIQ_RAW_STATUS | `0x40020060` |

   Both masked results satisfy the claim. **None of these fields sets bits 8–15**, however. Consequently, the byte and halfword read expectations remain identical.

   **(d) Existing semantic checks and names are preserved.** Comparing the frozen source versions and enumerating the archived FIX names found no removed names. The intentional exception is IDENTITY’s expectation, `333 → 491`.

   Enumeration produced:

   - X6: `{36,36,18,36,36}` rows in the listed five-site order, totaling 162.
   - X7: 35 rows per state, totaling 105.
   - X9: 23 rows per state, totaling 69.
   - X1–X8: `{12,11,3,10,2,162,105,6}`, totaling 311.
   - Added names: X6 54, X7 35, X9 69; `333 + 54 + 35 + 69 = 491`.

   Generated X6/X7/X9 name sets matched the archived FIX output exactly.

2. **Q2 — a small escape within the stated bound.**

   At [IRQ_RAW_STATUS’s direction test](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2r/frozen/dev_footbridge.c:584), consider adding the conjunct:

   ```c
   if (writeflag == MEM_READ && !d->warned_irq_raw_w)
   ```

   Once that site is latched, a legal read skips both branches and reaches the common tail with `odata == 0`. It silently returns the wrong value when `irq_status` is nonzero.

   I predict all 491 rows remain green:

   - X9, X8, X2 and P0 check IRQ_RAW_STATUS values with its latch clear.
   - P7’s post-decline value checks cover IRQ_STATUS and FIQ_RAW_STATUS, **not IRQ_RAW_STATUS**.
   - X3 and X7 exercise latched IRQ_RAW_STATUS reads without checking their values.
   - Declined accesses behave unchanged.

   Specifically, **`X7 legal len 4 read of IRQ_RAW_STATUS (wide) re-arms NO latch`** stays green while returning `0` instead of `0x80010018`.

   This is a source-derived prediction, not a mutant execution. The confirming measurement would be this single edit against the frozen detector, expecting `491 rows, 0 failures`.

3. **Q3 — more than five instances, but still not class closure.**

   One completely absent cell is:

   `IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR × WRITE × width 1 × data 0 × all-zero × latch-clear`.

   Its width-2 companion is also absent. X7 and X9 issue enable writes only at width 4. These accesses belong to the same edited case and legal direction, inside §C.2.

   Latch combinations also need the producer’s domain. Each latch is initialized to zero and independently assigned one. Using bits 0–4 for IRQ_STATUS, IRQ_RAW_STATUS, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, FIQ_STATUS and FIQ_RAW_STATUS, the detector’s sequences reach:

   `{00,01,02,03,04,07,08,0f,10,1f}`.

   Legal accesses occur with masks `{00,1f}`. A legal IRQ_RAW_STATUS read with only its own latch set, mask `02`, is absent.

   Nor do the three field states establish coverage of arbitrary single-field predicates. For example, `irq_enable=0x100` is producible through its existing Set arm and is never seeded here; this is not an excluded joint predicate over two fields.

   Before shipment, extend the legal-access checks to verify their results and preservation with latches set, cover narrow Clear writes, and make the contract explicit about remaining state sampling.

4. **Q4 — records need corrections.**

   - The rewritten CONTRACT’s class claim and X9’s unqualified “EVERY LEGAL ACCESS” overstate coverage for the reasons above.
   - “Bits 8–31 … are exercised” should specify **some bits above bit 7**; bits 8–15 remain zero.
   - The unchanged [X8 comment](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2r/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:937), “every seed above fits in ONE byte,” becomes false when the wide X6/X7 seeds land.
   - X9’s historical “returned 0” wording should specify **read data**; Q5 concerns a write, and the handler’s return code remains 1.

   **Another factual correction:** `CARRIED_KILLS.txt` contains 59 entries: FIX, 49 historical mutants, and nine new cases `{Q1,Q2,Q3,Q4,Q5,W1,W2,T1,T2}`. It does not contain 59 variants with TABLE4 comparands. I independently compared the archived historical red-name sets, applied the documented rename, and found no lost named red rows. `{M1,M1b,M1c,M1d,M1e}` instead have process-death records without row summaries.

   PRETABLE’s observed timestamp matches 14:19:25 Chicago time. A snapshot and timestamp cannot establish “never amended.”

5. **Q5 — omitting the guest reset reads is not my shipment blocker.**

   Offline rows can carry the agreed handler-level floor once their gaps are repaired. Guest reset reads would add integration coverage of actual initialization, MMIO dispatch and diagnostic behavior, which the direct-call detector cannot establish. Under the disclosed guest scope, that additional coverage is useful but does not independently require blocking this repair.

   - Seeded: `{0x10, 0x10, 0x10, 0}`.
   - All-zero: `{0, 0, 0, 0}`.
   - Wide: `{0x10010, 0x10010, 0x10010, 0x10000}`.

   The handler leaves the other three fields untouched. X9 asserts these outcomes correctly.

   **(c) Yes, each wide field has bits above byte zero.** Enumerating the actual set bits:

   | Field | Set bits |
   |---|---|
   | `irq_status` | `{3, 4, 16, 31}` |
   | `irq_enable` | `{4, 5, 16}` |
   | `fiq_status` | `{5, 6, 17, 30}` |
   | `fiq_enable` | `{6, 7, 17}` |

   The four wide status-read results are:

   | Register | Calculation/result |
   |---|---|
   | IRQ_STATUS | `0x80010018 & 0x00010030 = 0x00010010` |
   | IRQ_RAW_STATUS | `0x80010018` |
   | FIQ_STATUS | `0x40020060 & 0x000200c0 = 0x00020040` |
   | FIQ_RAW_STATUS | `0x40020060` |

   Both masked results satisfy the claim. **None of these fields sets bits 8–15**, however. Consequently, the byte and halfword read expectations remain identical.

   **(d) Existing semantic checks and names are preserved.** Comparing the frozen source versions and enumerating the archived FIX names found no removed names. The intentional exception is IDENTITY’s expectation, `333 → 491`.

   Enumeration produced:

   - X6: `{36,36,18,36,36}` rows in the listed five-site order, totaling 162.
   - X7: 35 rows per state, totaling 105.
   - X9: 23 rows per state, totaling 69.
   - X1–X8: `{12,11,3,10,2,162,105,6}`, totaling 311.
   - Added names: X6 54, X7 35, X9 69; `333 + 54 + 35 + 69 = 491`.

   Generated X6/X7/X9 name sets matched the archived FIX output exactly.

2. **Q2 — a small escape within the stated bound.**

   At [IRQ_RAW_STATUS’s direction test](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2r/frozen/dev_footbridge.c:584), consider adding the conjunct:

   ```c
   if (writeflag == MEM_READ && !d->warned_irq_raw_w)
   ```

   Once that site is latched, a legal read skips both branches and reaches the common tail with `odata == 0`. It silently returns the wrong value when `irq_status` is nonzero.

   I predict all 491 rows remain green:

   - X9, X8, X2 and P0 check IRQ_RAW_STATUS values with its latch clear.
   - P7’s post-decline value checks cover IRQ_STATUS and FIQ_RAW_STATUS, **not IRQ_RAW_STATUS**.
   - X3 and X7 exercise latched IRQ_RAW_STATUS reads without checking their values.
   - Declined accesses behave unchanged.

   Specifically, **`X7 legal len 4 read of IRQ_RAW_STATUS (wide) re-arms NO latch`** stays green while returning `0` instead of `0x80010018`.

   This is a source-derived prediction, not a mutant execution. The confirming measurement would be this single edit against the frozen detector, expecting `491 rows, 0 failures`.

3. **Q3 — more than five instances, but still not class closure.**

   One completely absent cell is:

   `IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR × WRITE × width 1 × data 0 × all-zero × latch-clear`.

   Its width-2 companion is also absent. X7 and X9 issue enable writes only at width 4. These accesses belong to the same edited case and legal direction, inside §C.2.

   Latch combinations also need the producer’s domain. Each latch is initialized to zero and independently assigned one. Using bits 0–4 for IRQ_STATUS, IRQ_RAW_STATUS, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, FIQ_STATUS and FIQ_RAW_STATUS, the detector’s sequences reach:

   `{00,01,02,03,04,07,08,0f,10,1f}`.

   Legal accesses occur with masks `{00,1f}`. A legal IRQ_RAW_STATUS read with only its own latch set, mask `02`, is absent.

   Nor do the three field states establish coverage of arbitrary single-field predicates. For example, `irq_enable=0x100` is producible through its existing Set arm and is never seeded here; this is not an excluded joint predicate over two fields.

   Before shipment, extend the legal-access checks to verify their results and preservation with latches set, cover narrow Clear writes, and make the contract explicit about remaining state sampling.

4. **Q4 — records need corrections.**

   - The rewritten CONTRACT’s class claim and X9’s unqualified “EVERY LEGAL ACCESS” overstate coverage for the reasons above.
   - “Bits 8–31 … are exercised” should specify **some bits above bit 7**; bits 8–15 remain zero.
   - The unchanged [X8 comment](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2r/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:937), “every seed above fits in ONE byte,” becomes false when the wide X6/X7 seeds land.
   - X9’s historical “returned 0” wording should specify **read data**; Q5 concerns a write, and the handler’s return code remains 1.

   **Another factual correction:** `CARRIED_KILLS.txt` contains 59 entries: FIX, 49 historical mutants, and nine new cases `{Q1,Q2,Q3,Q4,Q5,W1,W2,T1,T2}`. It does not contain 59 variants with TABLE4 comparands. I independently compared the archived historical red-name sets, applied the documented rename, and found no lost named red rows. `{M1,M1b,M1c,M1d,M1e}` instead have process-death records without row summaries.

   PRETABLE’s observed timestamp matches 14:19:25 Chicago time. A snapshot and timestamp cannot establish “never amended.”

5. **Q5 — omitting the guest reset reads is not my shipment blocker.**

   Offline rows can carry the agreed handler-level floor once their gaps are repaired. Guest reset reads would add integration coverage of actual initialization, MMIO dispatch and diagnostic behavior, which the direct-call detector cannot establish. Under the disclosed guest scope, that additional coverage is useful but does not independently require blocking this repair.

Ran read-only hashes, source diffing, Python arithmetic/address/name enumeration, and archived red-row comparisons. Ran no gate, battery, build or mutant.
